线性牙釉质发育不全的临床见解:病例报告

Clinical Insights Into Linear Enamel Hypoplasia: A Case Report
作者： Gowda Venkatesha R, Rajaram Mohan K
摘要中文翻译釉质生成是一次性过程,任何异常都可能导致永久性牙釉质异常。后两者具体称为 LEH [4]。LEH 的评估方法表 1 列出了 LEH 的各种评估方法。表 1 确定线性牙釉质发育不全 (LEH) 的各种方法。该表经作者许可修改和创建 [3-11]。作者年份方法计算方法优点缺点Massler M 和 Schour I [3]1941 年图表方法年龄于LEH 形成 1⁄4 = - [1/速度 x LEH 与 CEJ 的距离] + 冠完成时的年龄大量生物考古学家使用它们。观察到的特征延伸超出了它们出现的牙冠特定三分之一的单个周周的通常宽度。它依赖于牙釉质表面的深度。牙釉质表面上牙石的积累可能会导致持久的正残留。线性牙釉质发育不全 (LEH) 是牙釉质无法正确形成或发生畸变,导致牙齿表面牙釉质条带减少。许多原因,包括遗传、营养不良和儿童疾病,都可能导致 LEH,而 LEH 只影响新生儿系。LEH是一种环境发育不全,具有许多对称性和环状异常。与其他人体组织不同,牙釉质无法恢复。牙釉质发育不全的后果因疾病严重程度、病因相互作用以及患者就诊时的年龄而异。本文提供了有关 LEH 的病因、流行病学、临床特征和鉴别 诊断 的信息。讨论定义LEH 是指牙冠表面的可见改变,可以在显微镜下或用肉眼观察到。它表现为不同宽度和深度的带...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨与儿童疾病和营养挑战相关的线性牙釉质发育不全(LEH)的临床特征和潜在原因。
设计与对象
该研究是一项 病例报告 项目,涉及一名 16 岁女性,她的上颌前牙唇面存在线性皱纹状缺损,重点关注早年压力和营养不良的影响。
核心方法
该研究利用临床检查方法来识别牙釉质缺陷,包括测量发育不全的区域以及使用修改后的 z 分数来评估牙周间距的偏差。
主要结果
病例报告在上颌前牙的唇面上发现了一个线性皱纹状缺损,有证据表明该缺损可能与断奶阶段的生理应激和含有受污染的海洋食品的高风险饮食有关。
解释与创新
作者将 LEH 的存在解释为生命早期压力和营养不良的一个指标,强调了其在识别弱势群体以采取有针对性的干预措施方面的潜在作用。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,LEH 可以作为评估生命早期营养和健康挑战的有价值的标记,这可能为高危人群的食品补充剂和疾病预防的公共卫生策略提供参考。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于单个 病例报告,可能无法代表更广泛的人群。该证据基于临床观察,强度中等。

Clinical Insights Into Linear Enamel Hypoplasia: A Case Report

Authors: Gowda Venkatesha R, Rajaram Mohan K
Original abstract (English)
Amelogenesis is a one-time procedure, and any abnormality might result in permanent enamel abnormalities. The latter two are specifically known as LEH [4].Methods of assessment of LEHThe various methods of assessment of LEH are presented in Table 1.Table 1Various methods to determine linear enamel hypoplasia (LEH).The table was modified and created with permission from the authors [3-11].AuthorYearMethodMethod of CalculationAdvantageDisadvantagesMassler M and Schour I [3]1941Chart methodAge at LEH formation 1⁄4 = - [1/velocity x distance of LEH from CEJ] + age at crown completionA significant number of bioarchaeologists use them. The observed features extend beyond the usual width of a single perikymata for the specific third of the tooth's crown in which they appear.It relies on the depth of the enamel surface.The accumulation of calculus on the surface of the enamel might result in enduring positive residuals. Linear enamel hypoplasia (LEH) is a failure or aberration of enamel to form correctly, resulting in bands of decreased enamel on the tooth surface. Many reasons, including genetics, malnutrition, and childhood diseases, can cause LEH, which solely affects the neonatal line. LEH is a kind of environmental hypoplasia with many symmetrical and ring-like abnormalities. Enamel, unlike other human tissues, cannot be restored. The consequences of enamel hypoplasia vary based on illness severity, etiological agent interaction, and patient age upon presentation. The article provides information on the etiology, epidemiology, clinical features, and differential diagnosis of LEH. DiscussionDefinitionLEH refers to a visible alteration of the tooth crown surface, which may be observed under a microscope or with the naked eye. It appears as bands of different widths and dept...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to explore the clinical features and potential causes of linear enamel hypoplasia (LEH) in relation to childhood illnesses and nutritional challenges.
Design and population
The study is a case report involving a 16-year-old female who presented with a linear furrow-like defect on the labial surface of her maxillary anterior teeth, with a focus on the impact of early-life stress and malnutrition.
Core methods
The study utilized a clinical examination approach to identify enamel defects, including the measurement of hypoplastic areas and the use of a modified z-score to assess deviations in perikymata spacing.
Main results
The case report identified a linear furrow-like defect on the labial surface of the maxillary anterior teeth, with evidence suggesting that the defect may be linked to physiological stress during the weaning stage and a high-risk diet containing contaminated marine food.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the presence of LEH as an indicator of early-life stress and malnutrition, highlighting its potential role in identifying vulnerable populations for targeted interventions.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that LEH can serve as a valuable marker for assessing early-life nutritional and health challenges, which may inform public health strategies for food supplementation and disease prevention in at-risk groups.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to a single case report, which may not be representative of broader populations. The evidence is based on clinical observations and is considered moderate in strength.

多发性硬化症和病毒:《危险关系》。

Multiple Sclerosis and Viruses: « Liaisons Dangereuses ».
作者： Samanta Plavina, Daniela Malakovska, Jolanta Kalnina, Ilva Trapina, Elina Leonova, Mohammed Habib, Susanne Schifmann, Jegors Paramonovs, Nikolajs Sjakste, Yegor Vassetzky
摘要中文翻译多发性硬化症(MS)是一种复杂的、多因素的神经退行性疾病,其病因仍部分不明。与MS发病相关的风险因素包括遗传易感性和多种环境应激因素;然而,最近病毒感染引起了广泛关注,被认为是重要的环境诱因。尽管已知EB病毒(EBV)与MS易感性之间存在明确的关联,但单一病原体模型无法清晰描述驱动该疾病分子级联反应的机制,这表明从感染到自身免疫反应的转变可能涉及不止一种病毒。在本综述中,我们探讨了不同神经嗜性病毒之间的相互作用可能在MS中发挥协同致病作用的假设,这一领域目前在文献中研究较少。我们总结了不同病毒在MS发病中作用的矛盾证据,包括疱疹病毒科(人类巨细胞病毒、EB病毒、单纯疱疹病毒HSV-1和HSV-2,以及水痘-带状疱疹病毒)、副粘病毒科(麻疹病毒病毒和风疹病毒)、流感病毒、SARS-CoV-2以及人类内源性逆转录病毒(HERVs)。这些病毒可能通过在共享分子通路中的相互作用、免疫调节以及激活潜伏感染,共同影响MS中特征性的炎症和脱髓鞘过程。本综述评估了病毒相互作用在MS病因中的作用,通过这些病原体的累积和交互效应。现有的流行病学、免疫学和实验数据表明,病毒的共同暴露可能在遗传易感个体中协同影响MS的易感性。明确这些病毒相互作用为识别新型生物标志物和通过靶向控制病毒网络开发预防策略提供了新的研究方向。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了 麻疹病毒 在多发性硬化症 (MS) 病因学中的潜在作用,重点关注其与其他嗜神经病毒的相互作用以及这些病毒如何影响 MS 易感性。核心问题是,包括 麻疹病毒 在内的病毒共同暴露是否会协同作用,从而影响遗传易感个体的 MS 发展。
证据基础与方法
该综述借鉴了流行病学、免疫学和实验数据,对多发性硬化症中病毒相互作用的文献进行了综合分析。它没有指定特定的数据库或搜索日期,但它包括检查 麻疹病毒 和其他相关病毒在多发性硬化症中的作用的广泛研究。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,麻疹病毒 以及其他嗜神经病毒可能通过共享分子途径、免疫调节和激活潜伏感染在 MS 的发展中发挥作用。它强调,从感染到自身免疫反应的转变涉及多种病毒因子,而不仅仅是单一病原体。
争议与缺口
该综述承认,麻疹病毒 在 MS 中的作用仍未得到充分研究,并且关于其具体贡献的证据相互矛盾。它还指出,麻疹病毒 与其他病原体相互作用影响 MS 易感性的机制尚未完全了解。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,表征病毒相互作用,包括涉及 麻疹病毒 的病毒相互作用,为识别新型生物标志物和通过有针对性地控制病毒网络制定预防策略提供了一个新的研究方向。

Multiple Sclerosis and Viruses: « Liaisons Dangereuses ».

Authors: Samanta Plavina, Daniela Malakovska, Jolanta Kalnina, Ilva Trapina, Elina Leonova, Mohammed Habib, Susanne Schifmann, Jegors Paramonovs, Nikolajs Sjakste, Yegor Vassetzky
Original abstract (English)
Multiple sclerosis (MS) is a complex, multifactorial neurodegenerative disease with an aetiology that is still partially elusive. Several risk factors are associated with MS development including genetic predisposition and various environmental stressors; however, recently, viral infections have gained significant attention as a crucial environmental trigger. Despite the well-established link between Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) and MS susceptibility, a single-pathogen model fails to clearly characterise the molecular cascades driving the disease, suggesting that the transition from infection to autoimmune response involves more than one viral agent. In this review we examine the speculation that the interactions among different neurotropic viruses could serve a collective causative role in MS, which is under-researched in the literature at present. We summarise contradictory evidence on the role of different viral agents, including the Herpesviridae family (Human Cytomegalovirus, EBV, Herpes Simplex Viruses HSV-1 and HSV-2, and Varicella Zoster Virus), Paramyxoviridae family (Measles Virus and Rubella Virus), Influenza virus, SARS-CoV-2, and Human Endogenous Retroviruses (HERVs). Through interplay within shared molecular pathways, immune modulation, and activation of latent infections, these viruses may collectively influence the inflammatory and demyelinating processes that characterise MS. This review evaluates the etiological role of viral interplay in MS through the cumulative and interactive effects of these pathogens. The available epidemiological, immunological, and experimental data converge on the concept that viral co-exposures act synergistically to shape MS susceptibility in genetically predisposed individuals. Characterising these viral interactions presents a new research direction for the identification of novel biomarkers and the development of preventative strategies through the targeted control of viral networks.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review explores the potential role of measles virus in the aetiology of multiple sclerosis (MS), focusing on its interactions with other neurotropic viruses and how these may influence MS susceptibility. The central question is whether viral co-exposures, including measles virus, act synergistically to shape MS development in genetically predisposed individuals.
Evidence base and method
The review is a comprehensive analysis of the literature on viral interactions in MS, drawing on epidemiological, immunological, and experimental data. It does not specify particular databases or search dates, but it includes a broad range of studies examining the role of measles virus and other related viruses in MS.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that measles virus, along with other neurotropic viruses, may play a role in the development of MS through shared molecular pathways, immune modulation, and activation of latent infections. It highlights that the transition from infection to autoimmune response involves multiple viral agents, not just a single pathogen.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges that the role of measles virus in MS remains under-researched and that there is contradictory evidence regarding its specific contributions. It also notes that the mechanisms by which measles virus interacts with other pathogens to influence MS susceptibility are not yet fully understood.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that characterising viral interactions, including those involving measles virus, presents a new research direction for identifying novel biomarkers and developing preventative strategies through targeted control of viral networks.

健康儿童中含有水痘的 疫苗接种 后的不良事件:系统评价和荟萃分析

Adverse Events Following Varicella-Containing Vaccination in Healthy Children: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis
作者： Hao-Yang Hu, Yeran Jiaerken, Chao-Fan Wang, Hai Fang
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
含有水痘的疫苗大幅减轻了全球水痘的负担;然而,关于儿童 疫苗接种 不良事件的综合证据对于 疫苗 安全监测、疫苗 信心和免疫政策仍然很重要。
证据基础与方法
我们对评估 12 个月以上和 <12 岁健康儿童的单价水痘 疫苗 或 麻疹–腮腺炎–风疹–水痘 (MMRV) 疫苗 的随机对照试验 (RCT) 进行了系统回顾和荟萃分析。从2000年1月1日至2026年7月17日检索了八个数据库。使用具有随机效应的广义线性混合模型估计了接种疫苗的儿童中发病率的汇总比例。使用 Cochrane 偏倚风险 2 工具评估偏倚风险,并使用 GRADE 方法评估证据的确定性。
共识与结论
Forty-three RCTs involving 31,915 vaccinated children were included. Fever was the most frequently reported common adverse event, with a pooled incidence of 11.58% (95% confidence interval [CI], 6.79–19.07%). Pooled incidences were 2.74% (95% CI, 1.10–6.67%) for redness/erythema, 2.19% (95% CI, 0.93–5.06%) for pain, and 0.82% (95% CI, 0.33–1.98%) for swelling. Compared with monovalent varicella vaccines, MMRV vaccines showed higher pooled incidences of fever (43.44% vs. 5.29%), redness/erythema (16.50% vs. 0.69%), pain (12.27% vs. 0.91%), and swelling (7.02% vs. 0.30%); however, these estimates were based on between-study comparisons and should not be interpreted as causal effects of vaccine type.
争议与缺口
比较 MMRV 疫苗 与单价水痘 疫苗 的估计值基于研究间比较,不应解释为 疫苗 类型的因果效应。该评价没有报告特定亚组或地区不良事件的发病率,并且罕见严重不良事件的证据有限。
科研与实践启示
监测 的持续安全性仍然很重要,特别是对于罕见的严重不良事件。

Adverse Events Following Varicella-Containing Vaccination in Healthy Children: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis

Authors: Hao-Yang Hu, Yeran Jiaerken, Chao-Fan Wang, Hai Fang
Original abstract (English)
Background: Varicella-containing vaccines have substantially reduced the burden of varicella worldwide; however, comprehensive evidence on adverse events following vaccination in children remains important for vaccine safety monitoring, vaccine confidence and immunization policy. We aimed to systematically evaluate the incidence of adverse events following licensed varicella-containing vaccination in healthy children. Methods: We conducted a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (RCTs) evaluating monovalent varicella vaccines or measles–mumps–rubella–varicella (MMRV) vaccines in healthy children aged ≥12 months and <12 years. Eight databases were searched from 1 January 2000 to 17 July 2026. Studies using active safety monitoring and reporting extractable adverse event counts with corresponding denominators were included. Pooled incidence proportions among vaccinated children were estimated using generalized linear mixed models with random effects. Subgroup analyses were performed by vaccine type, vaccination dose, and World Health Organization region. Risk of bias was assessed using the Cochrane Risk of Bias 2 tool, and certainty of evidence was evaluated using the GRADE approach. Results: Forty-three RCTs involving 31,915 vaccinated children were included. Fever was the most frequently reported common adverse event, with a pooled incidence of 11.58% (95% confidence interval [CI], 6.79–19.07%). Pooled incidences were 2.74% (95% CI, 1.10–6.67%) for redness/erythema, 2.19% (95% CI, 0.93–5.06%) for pain, and 0.82% (95% CI, 0.33–1.98%) for swelling. Compared with monovalent varicella vaccines, MMRV vaccines showed higher pooled incidences of fever (43.44% vs. 5.29%), redness/erythema (16.50% vs. 0.69%), pain (12.27% vs. 0.91%), and swelling (7.02% vs. 0.30%); however, these estimates were based on between-study comparisons and should not be interpreted as causal effects of vaccine type. Rare serious adverse events were uncommon, including febrile seizures (0.0989%) and anaphylaxis (0.0343%), with no observed cases of encephalitis. Conclusions: Varicella-containing vaccines were generally well tolerated in children, with most observed adverse events being uncommon and rare serious adverse events infrequently reported. Continued safety surveillance remains important, particularly for rare serious adverse events.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Varicella-containing vaccines have substantially reduced the burden of varicella worldwide; however, comprehensive evidence on adverse events following vaccination in children remains important for vaccine safety monitoring, vaccine confidence and immunization policy.
Evidence base and method
We conducted a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (RCTs) evaluating monovalent varicella vaccines or measles–mumps–rubella–varicella (MMRV) vaccines in healthy children aged ≥12 months and <12 years. Eight databases were searched from 1 January 2000 to 17 July 2026. Pooled incidence proportions among vaccinated children were estimated using generalized linear mixed models with random effects. Risk of bias was assessed using the Cochrane Risk of Bias 2 tool, and certainty of evidence was evaluated using the GRADE approach.
Consensus and conclusions
Forty-three RCTs involving 31,915 vaccinated children were included. Fever was the most frequently reported common adverse event, with a pooled incidence of 11.58% (95% confidence interval [CI], 6.79–19.07%). Pooled incidences were 2.74% (95% CI, 1.10–6.67%) for redness/erythema, 2.19% (95% CI, 0.93–5.06%) for pain, and 0.82% (95% CI, 0.33–1.98%) for swelling. Compared with monovalent varicella vaccines, MMRV vaccines showed higher pooled incidences of fever (43.44% vs. 5.29%), redness/erythema (16.50% vs. 0.69%), pain (12.27% vs. 0.91%), and swelling (7.02% vs. 0.30%); however, these estimates were based on between-study comparisons and should not be interpreted as causal effects of vaccine type.
Controversies and gaps
The estimates comparing MMRV vaccines with monovalent varicella vaccines were based on between-study comparisons and should not be interpreted as causal effects of vaccine type. The review did not report on the incidence of adverse events in specific subgroups or regions, and the evidence for rare serious adverse events was limited.
Research and practice implications
Continued safety surveillance remains important, particularly for rare serious adverse events.

拆分时间表:对童年 疫苗接种 单抗原与组合 疫苗 完成度和及时性的系统回顾和荟萃分析

Splitting the Schedule: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Childhood Vaccination Completion and Timeliness With Single-Antigen Versus Combination Vaccines
作者： Kayla Hay, Kim W. Benner
摘要中文翻译描述/摘要 最近的联邦政策提出了“分割”儿童 疫苗 时间表,用单抗原 疫苗 替代推荐的组合产品,包括含有 DTaP 的五价/六价 疫苗 和 MMR。鉴于 疫苗 在美国交付的当前证据,这一转变值得审查。个别 队列研究 和调查研究报告称,组合产品与更高的系列完成度和更按时收到 疫苗接种 相关(Kurosky 等人,Human 疫苗 和免疫疗法,2017)。最近,以色列的混合方法 队列研究 将单一抗原的使用与明显的延迟和未完成联系起来(Sales 等人,疫苗,2026)。估计的大小各不相同,据我们所知,不存在汇总效应大小。由于 麻疹 和百日咳 暴发 的复苏是由聚集的 under-疫苗接种 驱动的,这些发现尚未定量综合。随着儿童 疫苗 时间表的问题在护理点越来越多地出现,药剂师必须有能力提供基于证据的指导。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述研究了儿童 疫苗接种 时间表中用单抗原 疫苗 替代组合 疫苗 的影响,重点关注 麻疹病毒 和相关实体。核心问题是与组合 疫苗 相比,单抗原 疫苗 是否会影响 疫苗接种 的完成度和及时性。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 队列研究 和在美国进行的调查研究的系统分析。它包括单抗原和组合 疫苗 研究,重点是 疫苗 递送方法及其对系列完成和及时性的影响。
共识与结论
根据个体 队列研究 和调查研究的报告,组合 疫苗 与更高的系列完成度和更及时的 疫苗 相关(Kurosky 等人,Human 疫苗 和免疫治疗,2017)。
争议与缺口
单抗原 疫苗 对 疫苗接种 完成和及时性的影响的估计在大小上有所不同,并且证据中没有报告汇总效应大小。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,使用单抗原疫苗可能会导致疫苗接种系列的延迟和无法完成,这与公共卫生政策和临床实践相关。这些结果凸显了药剂师和医疗保健提供者需要基于证据的指导。

Splitting the Schedule: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Childhood Vaccination Completion and Timeliness With Single-Antigen Versus Combination Vaccines

Authors: Kayla Hay, Kim W. Benner
Original abstract (English)
Description/ Abstract Recent federal policy has advanced a “split” childhood vaccine schedule, substituting single-antigen vaccines for recommended combination products, including DTaP-containing pentavalent/hexavalent vaccines and MMR. This shift warrants examination in light of current evidence on vaccine delivery in the United States. Individual cohort and survey studies have reported that combination products are associated with higher series completion and more on-time receipt of vaccination (Kurosky et al., Human Vaccines & Immunotherapeutics, 2017). More recently, an Israeli mixed-methods cohort linked single-antigen use to marked delay and non-completion (Sales et al., Vaccines, 2026). Estimates vary in magnitude, and to our knowledge, no pooled effect size exists. With resurgent measles and pertussis outbreaks driven by clustered under-vaccination, these findings have not been quantitatively synthesized. As questions on the childhood vaccine schedule increasingly arise at the point of care, pharmacists must be equipped to offer evidence-based guidance.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the impact of substituting single-antigen vaccines for combination vaccines in the childhood vaccination schedule, focusing on measles virus and related entities. The central question is whether single-antigen vaccines affect vaccination completion and timeliness compared to combination vaccines.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of cohort and survey studies conducted in the United States. It includes both single-antigen and combination vaccine studies, with a focus on vaccine delivery methods and their effects on series completion and timeliness.
Consensus and conclusions
Combination vaccines are associated with higher series completion and more timely vaccination, as reported by individual cohort and survey studies (Kurosky et al., Human Vaccines & Immunotherapeutics, 2017).
Controversies and gaps
Estimates of the effect of single-antigen vaccines on vaccination completion and timeliness vary in magnitude, and no pooled effect size has been reported in the evidence.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that the use of single-antigen vaccines may lead to delays and non-completion of the vaccination series, which is relevant for public health policy and clinical practice. These results highlight the need for evidence-based guidance for pharmacists and healthcare providers.

评估受冲突影响的苏丹埃博拉病毒病 暴发 的风险和卫生系统的应对能力

Assessing the Risk for Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak and Health System Response Capacity in Conflict-Affected Sudan
作者： Damilare Michael Akintunde, Dawit Mulugeta Gebremedhin, Moutsem Ibrahim Abdulrhman Babeker, Ismail MNYM, Michael Adeyemi Lawal, Mohammed Nour Yousof Mohammed Ismail
摘要中文翻译背景:埃博拉病毒病(EVD)是一种严重的病毒性出血热,病死率很高。乌干达和刚果民主共和国持续存在的区域性 暴发,加上苏丹自 2023 年 4 月以来旷日持久的武装冲突,增加了输入和传播的脆弱性,使苏丹面临风险。冲突造成了广泛的卫生基础设施破坏、医护人员流离失所以及 监测 和实验室系统的破坏。本研究评估了苏丹发生埃博拉病毒病 暴发 的风险以及冲突背景下的国家应对能力。方法:我们使用 PubMed、Medline、Google Scholar、WHO、CDC、UNICEF 和 UN OCHA 报告审查了同行评审的灰色文献。没有时间限制。我们应用 SWOT 分析框架对影响受冲突影响的苏丹埃博拉病毒病预防、检测、准备和应对的因素进行分类。该分析重点关注卫生系统功能、监测、实验室能力、劳动力和人口脆弱性。结果:我们的研究发现了现有优势,包括国家埃博拉病毒病防范计划的可用性、应对霍乱、登革热、麻疹 和 COVID-19 的先前经验、通过健康集群进行的功能协调以及持续疾病 监测 系统(包括 EWARS 和基于事件的 监测)。发现的弱点包括卫生服务严重中断(48%的设施功能齐全)、卫生人力短缺和倦怠、实验室能力中断以及达尔富尔和科尔多凡受冲突影响地区的 监测 和准入限制。正在进行的卫生安全框架,包括国家卫生政策、国家卫生部门恢复计划和区域跨境举措、数字 监测 的扩展、实验室权力下放工作以及最近对卫生系统恢复的国际投资,为最佳准备和应对提供了重要机会。已确定的威胁包括持续的武装冲突和不安全、大规模境内流离失所和跨境流动、人道主义准入限制以及资金不稳定。地理风险综合表明,达尔富尔面临延迟检测和沉默 传播 的最高风险,科尔多凡充当 传播 走廊,喀土穆面临城市放大的最高风险。结论:由于潜在的病毒输入、因冲突而被削弱的卫生系统和大规模流离失所,苏丹面临着中度至高度的埃博拉病毒病 暴发 风险。主要优先事项:加强冲突地区的 EWARS、分散实验室能力、通过强大的 IPC 恢复卫生设施、建设劳动力能力以及采取流动性知情的准备。和平和国际支持对于有效应对埃博拉病毒病仍然至关重要。

范围与问题
埃博拉病毒病(EVD)是一种严重的病毒性出血热,病死率很高。本研究评估了苏丹发生埃博拉病毒病 暴发 的风险以及冲突背景下的国家应对能力。
证据基础与方法
我们使用 PubMed、Medline、Google Scholar、WHO、CDC、UNICEF 和 UN OCHA 报告审查了同行评审的灰色文献。没有时间限制。
共识与结论
由于潜在的病毒输入、因冲突而被削弱的卫生系统和大规模流离失所,苏丹面临中度至高度埃博拉病毒病 暴发 风险。和平和国际支持对于有效应对埃博拉病毒病仍然至关重要。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据并未报告与 麻疹病毒 相关的具体争议或证据差距。
科研与实践启示
主要优先事项:加强冲突地区的 EWARS、分散实验室能力、通过强大的 IPC 恢复卫生设施、建设劳动力能力以及采取流动性知情的准备。

Assessing the Risk for Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak and Health System Response Capacity in Conflict-Affected Sudan

Authors: Damilare Michael Akintunde, Dawit Mulugeta Gebremedhin, Moutsem Ibrahim Abdulrhman Babeker, Ismail MNYM, Michael Adeyemi Lawal, Mohammed Nour Yousof Mohammed Ismail
Original abstract (English)
Background: Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) is a severe viral hemorrhagic fever with high case fatality rates. Ongoing regional outbreaks in Uganda and the DRC, combined with protracted armed conflict in Sudan since April 2023, increase vulnerability to importation and spread, putting Sudan at risk. Conflict has caused widespread health infrastructure destruction, healthcare worker displacement, and disruption of surveillance and laboratory systems. This study assesses Sudan’s risk of an EVD outbreak and national response capacity in the context of conflict. Methods: We reviewed peer-reviewed and grey literature using PubMed, Medline, Google Scholar, WHO, CDC, UNICEF, and UN OCHA reports. No time restrictions were applied. We applied a SWOT analysis framework to classify factors influencing EVD prevention, detection, preparedness, and response in conflict-affected Sudan. The analysis focused on health system functionality, surveillance, laboratory capacity, workforce, and population vulnerabilities. Results: Our study found existing strengths, including availability of national EVD preparedness plans, prior experience responding to cholera, dengue, measles, and COVID-19, functional coordination through the Health Cluster, and ongoing disease surveillance systems including EWARS and Event-Based Surveillance. Weaknesses identified include severe disruption of health services (48% of facilities fully functional), health workforce shortages and burnout, disruption of laboratory capacity, and surveillance and access constraints in conflict-affected areas of Darfur and Kordofan. The ongoing health security frameworks, including the National Health Policy, National Health Sector Recovery Plan, and regional cross-border initiatives, expansion of digital surveillance, laboratory decentralization efforts, and recent international investments in health system recovery, provide significant opportunities for optimal preparedness and response. Threats identified include continued armed conflict and insecurity, large-scale internal displacement and cross-border mobility, humanitarian access constraints, and funding instability. Geographic risk synthesis indicates Darfur is at the highest risk for delayed detection and silent transmission, Kordofan serves as a transmission corridor, and Khartoum faces the highest risk of urban amplification. Conclusion: Sudan faces a moderate to high EVD outbreak risk due to potential virus importation, a conflict-weakened health system, and mass displacement. Key priorities: strengthen EWARS in conflict zones, decentralize laboratory capacity, restore health facilities with strong IPC, build workforce capacity, and adopt mobility-informed preparedness. Peace and international support remain essential for effective EVD response.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) is a severe viral hemorrhagic fever with high case fatality rates. This study assesses Sudan’s risk of an EVD outbreak and national response capacity in the context of conflict.
Evidence base and method
We reviewed peer-reviewed and grey literature using PubMed, Medline, Google Scholar, WHO, CDC, UNICEF, and UN OCHA reports. No time restrictions were applied.
Consensus and conclusions
Sudan faces a moderate to high EVD outbreak risk due to potential virus importation, a conflict-weakened health system, and mass displacement. Peace and international support remain essential for effective EVD response.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report specific controversies or evidence gaps related to the measles virus.
Research and practice implications
Key priorities: strengthen EWARS in conflict zones, decentralize laboratory capacity, restore health facilities with strong IPC, build workforce capacity, and adopt mobility-informed preparedness.

基于微针的透皮给药:最新进展和未来前景

Microneedle-Based Transdermal Drug Delivery: Recent Advances and Future Prospects
作者： B.Jeelani, Angamuthu Raja, S.Buvaneshwari, K. H. Ibrahim Rahathullah, J.Jalshiya, K.Karthieswari, K.Maries, Raja Singh, A. Daisy, M.Sineha
摘要中文翻译微针 (MN) 系统正在成为一种多功能平台,可以在角质层上创建微米级的通路,同时限制对更深的疼痛感受器和血管的刺激。这种微创方法将透皮递送扩展到传统上适合被动贴剂的小、中等亲脂性分子之外,并能够施用肽、蛋白质、疫苗、难溶性药物、核酸制剂和局部作用的皮肤病药物。本综述严格审查了实心、涂层、空心、溶解、可分离和水凝胶形成的微针,重点是材料选择、针几何形状、药物定位和制造方法如何决定插入可靠性、剂量容量、释放动力学和产品稳定性。最新进展包括用于高有效负载的纯药物针尖、用于亲脂性分子的纳米悬浮液负载溶解阵列、与工程 储存宿主 相结合的水凝胶形成系统、用于持续皮内储库的快速分离针尖以及用于快速设计优化的增材制造。来自流感、麻疹-风疹和骨质疏松症项目的临床证据证实了微阵列贴片的转化潜力,尽管局部反应、剂量输送变异性和扩大规模仍然是重要的限制因素。关键质量属性包括形态、机械强度、插入深度、药物含量和分布、溶解或膨胀、渗透、无菌保证、包装完整性和用户施加的力。未来的进展将取决于综合的质量设计开发、强大的体外体内关系、气候适应性包装、人因工程和更清晰的组合产品监管途径。因此,微针不仅可以作为注射的替代品,而且可以作为局部、全身和响应性治疗的可编程接口。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
微针 (MN) 系统正在成为一种多功能平台,可以在角质层上创建微米级的通路,同时限制对更深的疼痛感受器和血管的刺激。这种微创方法将透皮递送扩展到传统上适合被动贴剂的小、中等亲脂性分子之外,并能够施用肽、蛋白质、疫苗、难溶性药物、核酸制剂和局部作用的皮肤病药物。
证据基础与方法
本综述严格审查了实心、涂层、空心、溶解、可分离和水凝胶形成的微针,重点是材料选择、针几何形状、药物定位和制造方法如何决定插入可靠性、剂量容量、释放动力学和产品稳定性。
共识与结论
最新进展包括用于高有效负载的纯药物针尖、用于亲脂性分子的纳米悬浮液负载溶解阵列、与工程 储存宿主 相结合的水凝胶形成系统、用于持续皮内储库的快速分离针尖以及用于快速设计优化的增材制造。来自流感、麻疹-风疹和骨质疏松症项目的临床证据证实了微阵列贴片的转化潜力,尽管局部反应、剂量输送变异性和扩大规模仍然是重要的限制因素。
争议与缺口
关键质量属性包括形态、机械强度、插入深度、药物含量和分布、溶解或膨胀、渗透、无菌保证、包装完整性和用户施加的力。未来的进展将取决于综合的质量设计开发、强大的体外体内关系、气候适应性包装、人因工程和更清晰的组合产品监管途径。
科研与实践启示
因此,微针不仅可以作为注射的替代品,而且可以作为局部、全身和响应性治疗的可编程接口。

Microneedle-Based Transdermal Drug Delivery: Recent Advances and Future Prospects

Authors: B.Jeelani, Angamuthu Raja, S.Buvaneshwari, K. H. Ibrahim Rahathullah, J.Jalshiya, K.Karthieswari, K.Maries, Raja Singh, A. Daisy, M.Sineha
Original abstract (English)
Microneedle (MN) systems are emerging as versatile platforms that create micron-scale pathways across the stratum corneum while limiting stimulation of deeper pain receptors and blood vessels. This minimally invasive approach expands transdermal delivery beyond the small, moderately lipophilic molecules traditionally suited to passive patches and enables administration of peptides, proteins, vaccines, poorly soluble drugs, nucleic-acid formulations and locally acting dermatological agents. This review critically examines solid, coated, hollow, dissolving, separable and hydrogel-forming microneedles, with emphasis on how material selection, needle geometry, drug localization and manufacturing method determine insertion reliability, dose capacity, release kinetics and product stability. Recent advances include pure-drug needle tips for high payloads, nanosuspension-loaded dissolving arrays for lipophilic molecules, hydrogel-forming systems coupled with engineered reservoirs, rapidly separating tips for sustained intradermal depots, and additive manufacturing for rapid design optimization. Clinical evidence from influenza, measles-rubella and osteoporosis programmes confirms the translational potential of microarray patches, although local reactions, dose delivery variability and scale-up remain important constraints. Critical quality attributes include morphology, mechanical strength, insertion depth, drug content and distribution, dissolution or swelling, permeation, sterility assurance, packaging integrity and user-applied force. Future progress will depend on integrated quality-by-design development, robust in vitro-in vivo relationships, climate-resilient packaging, human-factor engineering and clearer combination-product regulatory pathways. Microneedles are therefore positioned not merely as an alternative to injections, but as a programmable interface for localized, systemic and responsive therapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Microneedle (MN) systems are emerging as versatile platforms that create micron-scale pathways across the stratum corneum while limiting stimulation of deeper pain receptors and blood vessels. This minimally invasive approach expands transdermal delivery beyond the small, moderately lipophilic molecules traditionally suited to passive patches and enables administration of peptides, proteins, vaccines, poorly soluble drugs, nucleic-acid formulations and locally acting dermatological agents.
Evidence base and method
This review critically examines solid, coated, hollow, dissolving, separable and hydrogel-forming microneedles, with emphasis on how material selection, needle geometry, drug localization and manufacturing method determine insertion reliability, dose capacity, release kinetics and product stability.
Consensus and conclusions
Recent advances include pure-drug needle tips for high payloads, nanosuspension-loaded dissolving arrays for lipophilic molecules, hydrogel-forming systems coupled with engineered reservoirs, rapidly separating tips for sustained intradermal depots, and additive manufacturing for rapid design optimization. Clinical evidence from influenza, measles-rubella and osteoporosis programmes confirms the translational potential of microarray patches, although local reactions, dose delivery variability and scale-up remain important constraints.
Controversies and gaps
Critical quality attributes include morphology, mechanical strength, insertion depth, drug content and distribution, dissolution or swelling, permeation, sterility assurance, packaging integrity and user-applied force. Future progress will depend on integrated quality-by-design development, robust in vitro-in vivo relationships, climate-resilient packaging, human-factor engineering and clearer combination-product regulatory pathways.
Research and practice implications
Microneedles are therefore positioned not merely as an alternative to injections, but as a programmable interface for localized, systemic and responsive therapy.

探索实施基于社区的 监测 的推动因素和障碍,加强巴基斯坦 Mardan Khyber Pakhtunkhwa 地区的早期发现和响应

Exploring enablers and barriers for implementing community-based surveillance strengthening early detection and response in District Mardan Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Pakistan
作者： Zeeshan Iqbal Baig, Babar Tasneem Shaikh, Muhammad Salman
摘要中文翻译基于社区的 监测 (CBS) 通过社区参与从源头识别事件,从而在基于设施的报告系统之前检测到疾病 暴发,从而补充医疗机构以外的早期预警警报和响应。在巴基斯坦,登革热、麻疹 和其他传染病的反复出现凸显了在社区层面进行事件检测的必要性。尽管基于社区的 监测 在全球范围内得到倡导,但地区层面的实施仍然有限。CBS 的成功实施取决于对当地情况、利益相关者的准备情况和有利因素的了解。在开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省马尔丹区实施 CBS 之前,这项定性研究通过探讨多方利益相关者对障碍和促成因素的观点来评估实施准备情况。该研究涉及地区卫生官员、女性卫生工作者、设施工作人员和社区志愿者,以确定社区有效参与 暴发 检测和响应系统所需的背景因素和策略。2025 年 6 月至 8 月在巴基斯坦开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省马尔丹区进行了一项定性探索性研究。我们对地区官员(DHO、省协调员、3 名项目经理/官员)进行了 5 次关键知情人访谈 (KII),并根据利益相关者角色进行了 3 次焦点小组讨论 (FGD),其中包括女性保健工作者 (LHW,n = 17)、卫生机构工作人员 (HFS,n = 13) 和社区志愿者 (CV,n = 15),总共 n = 50 名参与者。使用半结构化指南收集数据,在同意的情况下进行录音,并使用 NVivo 12 中的归纳和演绎编码进行转录和分析。四个利益相关者群体之间的数据三角测量增强了研究结果的可信度。CBS 被认为在识别事件以及补充国家 监测 系统的预警警报和响应组件方面具有附加值。社区警报通常没有记录。参与者描述了 LHW 覆盖范围的感知差距,特别是在城郊和偏远地区。加强 CBS 实施包括积极主动的一线工作人员、强有力的社区参与以及增加数字工具的使用。挑战包括被动报告、有限的培训、数据集成薄弱以及对 疫苗接种 活动的不信任。与会者建议振兴村卫生委员会、统一实时报告、持续培训、加强反馈机制。马尔丹的哥伦比亚广播公司提供了一个有希望的基础,但仍然支离破碎。扩大 LHW 覆盖范围、加强报告系统以及重建与社区的信任是实现更有效的早期发现和应对的关键步骤。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究的重点是实施基于社区的 监测 (CBS),以早期发现和应对传染病,包括巴基斯坦开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省马尔丹区的 麻疹。核心问题是关于 CBS 实施的准备情况,特别是在确定社区有效参与 暴发 检测和响应系统的障碍和推动因素方面。
证据基础与方法
该研究是一项定性探索性研究,于 2025 年 6 月至 8 月在巴基斯坦开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省马尔丹区进行。数据是通过与地区官员进行的五次关键知情人访谈 (KII) 以及按利益相关者角色分层的三场焦点小组讨论 (FGD) 收集的,包括女性卫生工作者 (LHW,n = 17)、卫生机构工作人员 (HFS,n = 13) 和社区志愿者 (CV,n = 15),总共 n = 50 名参与者。使用 NVivo 12 中的归纳和演绎编码对数据进行分析,并对四个利益相关者群体进行数据三角测量,以增强研究结果的可信度。
共识与结论
基于社区的 监测 (CBS) 被认为是识别事件和补充国家 监测 系统的早期预警和响应组件的增值功能。然而,CBS 在马尔丹的实施仍然支离破碎,凸显了加强报告系统、增加 LHW 覆盖范围以及重建社区信任的必要性。
争议与缺口
该研究指出了被动报告、有限的培训、数据集成薄弱以及对 疫苗接种 活动的不信任等挑战。缺乏记录的社区警报,并且 LHW 覆盖范围存在差距,特别是在城郊和偏远地区。这些问题表明 CBS 实施中存在尚未解决的机制和证据差距。
科研与实践启示
为了提高CBS的有效性,研究建议振兴村卫生委员会,实施统一的实时报告系统,为一线工作人员提供持续培训,并建立更强有力的反馈机制。这些步骤对于提高该地区的早期检测和响应能力至关重要。

Exploring enablers and barriers for implementing community-based surveillance strengthening early detection and response in District Mardan Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Pakistan

Authors: Zeeshan Iqbal Baig, Babar Tasneem Shaikh, Muhammad Salman
Original abstract (English)
Community-based surveillance (CBS) compliments early warning alert and response beyond health facilities by identifying events at source through community engagement, enabling detection of disease outbreaks before facility-based reporting systems. In Pakistan, repeated outbreaks of dengue, measles and other infectious diseases highlight the need for event detection at community level. Although community-based surveillance is globally advocated, implementation at district level remains limited. Successful CBS implementation depends on understanding local contexts, stakeholder readiness, and enabling factors. Before implementing CBS in District Mardan, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, this qualitative study assesses implementation readiness by exploring multi-stakeholder perspectives on barriers and enablers. The study engages district health officials, Lady Health Workers, facility staff, and community volunteers to identify contextual factors and strategies needed for effective community participation in outbreak detection and response systems. A qualitative exploratory study was conducted from June to August 2025 in District Mardan, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan. We conducted five key informant interviews (KIIs) with district officials (DHO, Provincial Coordinator, 3 program managers/officers) and three focus group discussions (FGDs) stratified by stakeholder role including Lady Health Workers (LHWs, n = 17), health facility staff (HFS, n = 13), and community volunteers (CV, n = 15) totaling n = 50 participants. Data were collected using semi-structured guides, audio-recorded with consent, transcribed and analyzed using both inductive and deductive coding in NVivo 12. Data triangulation across four stakeholder groups strengthened credibility of findings. CBS was perceived a value addition in identification of events and to compliment early warning alerts and response component of national surveillance system. Community alerts often go undocumented. Participants described perceived gaps in LHW coverage, particularly in peri-urban and remote areas. Strengthening of CBS implementation included motivated frontline workers, strong community engagement and increasing use of digital tools. Challenges included passive reporting, limited training, weak data integration, and mistrust in vaccination campaigns. Participants recommended revitalizing village health committees, unified real-time reporting, continuous training, and stronger feedback mechanisms. CBS in Mardan provides a promising foundation but remains fragmented. Expanding LHW coverage, strengthening reporting systems, and rebuilding trust with communities are key steps toward more effective early detection and response.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the implementation of community-based surveillance (CBS) for early detection and response to infectious diseases, including measles, in District Mardan, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan. The central question is about the readiness for CBS implementation, particularly in identifying barriers and enablers for effective community participation in outbreak detection and response systems.
Evidence base and method
The study is a qualitative exploratory study conducted from June to August 2025 in District Mardan, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan. Data were collected through five key informant interviews (KIIs) with district officials and three focus group discussions (FGDs) stratified by stakeholder role, including Lady Health Workers (LHWs, n = 17), health facility staff (HFS, n = 13), and community volunteers (CV, n = 15), totaling n = 50 participants. Data were analyzed using both inductive and deductive coding in NVivo 12, with data triangulation across four stakeholder groups to strengthen the credibility of findings.
Consensus and conclusions
Community-based surveillance (CBS) was perceived as a value addition for identifying events and complementing the early warning alerts and response component of the national surveillance system. However, CBS implementation in Mardan remains fragmented, highlighting the need for strengthening reporting systems, increasing LHW coverage, and rebuilding trust with communities.
Controversies and gaps
The study identifies challenges such as passive reporting, limited training, weak data integration, and mistrust in vaccination campaigns. There is a lack of documented community alerts, and gaps in LHW coverage, particularly in peri-urban and remote areas, are noted. These issues suggest unresolved mechanisms and evidence gaps in the implementation of CBS.
Research and practice implications
To enhance the effectiveness of CBS, the study recommends revitalizing village health committees, implementing unified real-time reporting systems, providing continuous training for frontline workers, and establishing stronger feedback mechanisms. These steps are critical for improving early detection and response capabilities in the district.

健康专家敦促 麻疹 病例总数是去年的两倍

Measles cases now double last year's total as health experts urge vaccination
新闻简报截至 2026 年 10 月,美国已报告 4,588 例 麻疹 感染病例,比上一年的 2,289 例增加了一倍多。这种激增归因于 疫苗接种 覆盖范围的差距和错误信息的传播,特别是关于 MMR 疫苗 的错误信息。South Shore Health 的 Todd Ellerin 博士强调,由于 麻疹 率低和“不信任的流行”,麻疹 仍然是一个重大的公共卫生问题。他呼吁通过改善疾病预防控制中心和地方卫生部门之间的沟通和加强协调来重建公众信任。这种情况凸显了需要继续努力 疫苗接种 并解决 疫苗 的犹豫,因为 麻疹 被认为是世界上最具传染性的病毒。公共卫生官员敦促社区优先考虑 疫苗接种,以防止进一步的 暴发 并确保该病毒仍然是一个历史问题,而不是当前的健康威胁。

时间
南岸卫生局的托德·埃勒林 (Todd Ellerin) 表示,激增速度并不令人意外。美国 麻疹 感染人数目前为 4,588 例。
地点与对象
这是去年总数 2,289 的两倍多——而且这只是 10 月份。
事件
截至 2026 年 10 月,麻疹 病例总数比去年增加了一倍,报告感染人数为 4,588 例。这一激增归因于 麻疹 覆盖范围的差距和错误信息。
规模、影响与风险
美国的 麻疹 感染人数目前为 4,588 例,是去年总数 2,289 例的两倍多。这表明病例显着增加,特别是在疫苗接种不足的社区。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员敦促 疫苗接种 和加强沟通,以防止进一步传播。建议疾病预防控制中心加强其人员队伍并改善与当地卫生部门的协调。

Measles cases now double last year's total as health experts urge vaccination

News Brief
As of October 2026, the United States has reported 4,588 measles infections, more than double the 2,289 cases from the previous year. This surge is attributed to gaps in vaccination coverage and the spread of misinformation, particularly regarding the MMR vaccine. Dr. Todd Ellerin from South Shore Health emphasized that measles remains a significant public health concern due to low vaccination rates and 'epidemics of mistrust.' He called for rebuilding public trust through improved communication and stronger coordination between the CDC and local health departments. The situation highlights the need for continued vaccination efforts and addressing vaccine hesitancy, as measles is considered the most contagious virus in the world. Public health officials are urging the community to prioritize vaccination to prevent further outbreaks and ensure the virus remains a historical issue rather than a current health threat.
Original source text (English)
Measles cases now double last year's total as health experts urge vaccination Dr. Todd Ellerin from South Shore Health says speed of surge is not surprising Measles cases now double last year's total as health experts urge vaccination Dr. Todd Ellerin from South Shore Health says speed of surge is not surprising The number of measles infections in the United States is now 4,588. That's more than double last year's total of 2,289 --- and it's only October. For South Shore Health’s Dr. Todd Ellerin, the speed of the surge is not surprising since measles is considered the most contagious virus in the world. He says even small gaps in vaccination coverage can allow outbreaks to spread quickly, particularly in under-vaccinated communities. Why doctors are concerned Ellerin says the country has an effective tool to prevent measles, but vaccination rates remain a concern. “We have the right vaccine,” Ellerin said. “We have a measles problem because people are, you know, not accepting that vaccine.” He also pointed to what he called “epidemics of mistrust” and misinformation as challenges for public health officials. “In 2026, measles should be in our history books, not our hospitals," Ellerin said. What public health officials need to do Ellerin said rebuilding public trust will require more than providing information. He said the CDC should strengthen its public health workforce, improve coordination with state and local health departments and focus on clear, concise communication. He also criticized continued links between the MMR vaccine and autism, saying that connection has been disproven in multiple studies. With cases continuing to rise, he says vaccination and stronger public health communication remain key tools for preventing further spread

婴儿的母体 麻疹 保护可能不会持续到第一次 MMR 剂量

Infants' Maternal Measles Protection May Not Last Until First MMR Dose
新闻简报在 2026 年 IDWeek 年会上发表的一项研究发现,到 6 个月大时,大多数美国婴儿不再具有可检测到的 麻疹 母体中和抗体水平,这可能使他们在 12 个月大时首次接种 MMR 疫苗 疫苗之前变得脆弱。范德比尔特大学的医学博士、公共卫生硕士巴迪·克里奇 (Buddy Creech) 指出,到这个年龄,婴儿的抗体水平已降至保护相关水平以下。截至 2026 年 10 月 22 日,CDC 报告美国有 4,588 例 麻疹 病例,其中全国 3 例死亡,宾夕法尼亚州 5 例死亡。该研究强调需要重新考虑 疫苗接种 的时机,特别是在 麻疹 发行量上升的情况下,但警告说,较早的 疫苗接种 的有效性可能较低。尽管特朗普政府建议将 MMR 疫苗 成分分开,但美国儿科学会和 CDC 建议在 12-15 个月时注射第一剂。这些发现可能会影响未来的 麻疹 策略,以保护婴儿免受 麻疹 及其并发症的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究结果在 2026 年 10 月 23 日的 IDWeek 年会上报告,CDC 指出截至 2026 年 10 月 22 日已有 4,588 例 麻疹 病例。
地点与对象
该研究的重点是美国的婴儿,分析了 MOMI-Vax 和 Mix & Match 研究中的母婴对和成人。CDC 报告了美国的 麻疹 病例,几乎所有病例都发生在未接种疫苗或 麻疹 状态未知的个体中。
事件
一项回顾性分析发现,到 6 个月大时,大多数美国婴儿不再具有可检测到的 麻疹 母体中和抗体水平,这可能使他们在 12 个月时第一次接种 MMR 疫苗 之前面临风险。这是范德比尔特大学医学博士、公共卫生硕士 Buddy Creech 报道的。
规模、影响与风险
6 个月时,麻疹 菌株血清阳性的婴儿比例在埃德蒙斯顿 (A) 中为 8.7%,在马里兰.USA/18.18 (B3) 中为 19.6%,在密苏里.USA/15.18 (D8) 中为 17.4%。截至 2026 年 10 月 22 日,CDC 报告美国有 4,588 例 麻疹 病例,其中全国 3 例死亡,宾夕法尼亚州 5 例死亡。研究表明,如果 麻疹 延迟,婴儿可能容易受到 麻疹 及其并发症的影响。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Infants' Maternal Measles Protection May Not Last Until First MMR Dose

News Brief
A study presented at the IDWeek annual meeting in 2026 found that by 6 months of age, most U.S. infants no longer have detectable levels of maternal neutralizing antibodies for measles, potentially leaving them vulnerable before their first MMR vaccine dose at 12 months. Buddy Creech, MD, MPH, of Vanderbilt University, noted that infants' antibody levels dropped below the correlate of protection by this age. The CDC reported 4,588 measles cases in the U.S. as of October 22, 2026, with three national deaths and five in Pennsylvania. The study highlights the need to reconsider vaccination timing, especially with rising measles circulation, but warns that earlier vaccination may have lower effectiveness. The American Academy of Pediatrics and CDC recommend the first dose at 12-15 months, though the Trump administration has proposed separating MMR vaccine components. These findings may influence future vaccination strategies to protect infants from measles and its complications.
Original source text (English)
CME Healthcare Jobs Synopsi Edit Profile Manage Subscriptions CME Tracker Log Out Login Sign Up Specialties Back Allergy & Immunology Anesthesiology Cardiology Critical Care Dermatology Emergency Medicine Endocrinology Gastroenterology Genetics Geriatrics HIV / AIDS Hospital-Based Medicine Infectious Disease Nephrology Neurology Nursing OB / GYN Oncology / Hematology Ophthalmology Orthopedics Pain Management Pathology Pediatrics Pharmacy Primary Care Psychiatry Pulmonology Radiology Rheumatology Surgery Transplantation Urology Perspectives Health Policy Meetings Special Reports Break Room Back Wordosis Wordectomy Wordoscopy Weekly News Quiz Spell Check-Up All Games Resources Back Beyond Diagnosis Clinical Trial Finder Condition Centers DocAudio Making the Rounds Medical Journeys Society Partners Back AAD Reading Room ACR Reading Room AGA Reading Room ASCO Reading Room Endocrine Society Reading Room IDSA Reading Room OMA Reading Room Design Mode Design mode is active. Visit Storybook for more information. Meeting Coverage > IDWeek Infants' Maternal Measles Protection May Not Last Until First MMR Dose — But moving up the initial dose could come with its own trade-offs by Terrence Rudd, Staff Writer, MedPage Today October 23, 2026 • 3 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday WASHINGTON -- At 6 months of age, most U.S. infants no longer had detectable levels of maternal neutralizing antibodies for measles, potentially leaving them at risk of an infection months before their first dose of the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine at 12 months of age, according to a retrospective analysis. At birth, infants' median concentrations of neutralizing antibodies for three major circulating measles strains exceeded their mothers' levels. But those infant levels began consistently dropping below their moms' levels by 2 months of age. By 6 months, the infants' median neutralizing antibodies had fallen below 120 mIU/mL -- the study's correlate of measles protection -- for all three measles strains. At 6 months, the proportions of infants who were seropositive for the three measles strains were 8.7% for the Edmonston strain (A), 19.6% for Maryland.USA/18.18 (B3), and 17.4% for Missouri.USA/15.18 (D8), reported Buddy Creech, MD, MPH, of Vanderbilt University in Nashville, Tennessee. "What this means is that infants are going to be susceptible to measles virus infection sooner than maybe we previously thought," Creech said at the IDWeek annual meeting. "As measles circulation increases, infants are going to be an important vulnerable population to consider in our response." The findings could force a rethink about measles vaccination timing, he cautioned. "The immunologic impact of earlier vaccination, such as 9 or 10 months of age, should be evaluated in a well-powered clinical study," Creech noted. "At the very least, we're going to have to think about what it means to vaccinate in early toddler time -- 12 to 15 months -- and maybe accelerate the second dose." The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) recommends two doses of either the MMR vaccine or the combination MMR and varicella vaccine, with the first dose at age 12-15 months and the second dose at 4-6 years. CDC recommendations mirror those of AAP, though the agency controversially moved to recommend separate MMR and varicella vaccines for the first dose. Further complicating things, the Trump administration has proposed separating all components of the MMR vaccine into individual shots, and spacing them out whenever possible. The findings come as the U.S. grapples with its worst measles case total since 1991. As of Oct. 22, the CDC reported 4,588 cases, already doubling last year's total of 2,289. Nearly all (96%) of this year's cases have happened in people who were unvaccinated or whose vaccination status was unknown. While the CDC reports three measles deaths this year, Pennsylvania alone has reported five measles-associated deaths. Despite the spike, Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. and other Trump administration officials continue to fuel vaccine skepticism with disproven claims of vaccine-driven dangers, including from the MMR vaccine. Starting Sooner Comes With Costs Given the range of risks for infants, including subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, why not move up the first measles vaccine dose to 6 months? That's already happening when an infant is going to travel internationally or when they might be exposed to a local outbreak cluster, Creech noted. Measles vaccine effectiveness is very different, however, when the first dose is at 6 months rather than 9 months: 51% versus 83%, according to a 2019 meta-analysis, he cautioned. Vaccinating infants with a first dose at age 9 months might be warranted given higher measles virus circulation in the U.S., he noted, particularly if the U.S. loses its measles elimination status. "I think our data would imply that if we continue to wait until 12-15 months to vaccinate, we're going to have a subsection of our population at risk for measles and for its complications," Creech said. Creech's team analyzed measles binding and neutralizing antibodies in serum samples from maternal-infant pairs and adults in the MOMI-Vax and the Mix & Match studies, which explored COVID-19 vaccination. Most of the infants' 6-month neutralizing-antibody levels were at assays' lower detection limits, a finding that indicates their antibody titers were probably much lower than that, Creech noted. The same downward trend seen in neutralizing antibodies happened with the infants' binding antibody levels. By age 6 months, median levels were below the correlate protection levels. A secondary analysis showed that the immunologic protection given by moms to babies has waned over time as vaccination rates have risen. Among mothers who were born before the 1967 launch of the U.S. measles eradication program, infants' antibody levels remained high enough to likely confer protection for an estimated 6 months. Among mothers born after 1967 -- who didn't face measles infection or residual circulating measles -- infant protection fell to an estimated 4 months. A retrospective analysis found that by 6 months of age, most U.S. infants no longer had detectable levels of maternal neutralizing antibodies for measles, potentially leaving them at risk before their first MMR vaccine dose at 12 months. This was reported by Buddy Creech, MD, MPH, of Vanderbilt University.
Scale, impact and risk

The CDC reported 4,588 measles cases in the U.S. as of October 22, 2026, with three national deaths and five in Pennsylvania. The study suggests infants may be vulnerable to measles and its complications if vaccination is delayed.
Response, status and uncertainty
The findings could lead to a reconsideration of measles vaccination timing, with recommendations to potentially vaccinate infants as early as 6 months or 9 months. However, the effectiveness of the measles vaccine at 6 months is lower (51%) compared to 9 months (83%). The CDC and AAP recommend the first dose at 12-15 months, with the second at 4-6 years. The Trump administration has proposed separating MMR vaccine components, but this is not yet implemented.

在该州不断发展的 麻疹 暴发 中心,一切照常 - Inquirer.com

At the center of the state’s growing measles outbreak, it’s business as usual - Inquirer.com
新闻简报麻疹 暴发 目前正在影响宾夕法尼亚州米夫林县,自 7 月以来已有 193 例确诊病例,过去两周报告了 76 例新病例。麻疹 已导致至少四人与 麻疹 相关死亡,其中包括一名 18 岁的阿米什女孩,她上个月死于罕见的并发症。该县约有 5,500 名阿米什居民,感染率是全州最高的,七年级学生的 疫苗接种 感染率属于最低之列,为 77%。尽管 暴发 很严重,但几乎没有迹象表明公众对此表示关注或采取行动,当地人和官员基本上不理睬这个问题。暴发 已在阿米什社区内传播,他们对现代医学的传统观点阻碍了疫苗接种的努力。县验尸官确认阿米什女孩死于急性播散性脑脊髓炎,这是 麻疹 的一种严重神经系统并发症。公共卫生专家表示,报告的病例数可能被低估,情况仍然是一场日益严重的公共卫生危机。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

At the center of the state’s growing measles outbreak, it’s business as usual - Inquirer.com

News Brief
A measles outbreak is currently affecting Mifflin County, Pennsylvania, with 193 confirmed cases since July and 76 new cases reported in the last two weeks. The outbreak has led to at least four measles-related deaths, including an 18-year-old Amish girl who succumbed to a rare complication last month. The county, home to approximately 5,500 Amish residents, has the highest infection rate in the state, and vaccination rates for seventh-grade students are among the lowest, at 77%. Despite the severity of the outbreak, there are few signs of public concern or action, with locals and officials largely dismissing the issue. The outbreak has spread within the Amish community, and efforts to vaccinate are hindered by their traditional views on modern medicine. The county coroner confirmed the death of the Amish girl as a result of acute disseminated encephalomyelitis, a serious neurological complication of measles. Public health experts suggest that the reported case numbers are likely undercounts, and the situation remains a growing public health crisis.
Original source text (English)
The floats had been decorated, the public officials assembled, and now the parade was underway — a convoy of colorful trailers and pickup trucks rolling slowly through downtown. Firetrucks passed grandmothers in lawn chairs and young children, squealing with delight as they fetched candy tossed by the fistful from passing floats. It was Goose Day in Mifflin County — honoring an animal believed to bring good fortune and prosperity in the coming year — and everywhere you looked were geese: ceramic geese standing sentry on street corners, inflatable geese atop parade floats, goose meat soup over at the Square Cafe, $6.29 a cup. But the day’s joyous tone belied a grim reality. In the midst of the state’s deadliest measles outbreak in decades, Mifflin County — this rural enclave of 46,000 tucked into the rolling hills of the central Pennsylvania valley — had recently achieved a troubling distinction: It now counts the highest rate of infection of any county in the state. For weeks, the nation’s rapid spread of measles — once deemed eradicated in the U.S. thanks to the measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine — had been met with growing national alarm. Public health experts issued increasingly sober warnings. News outlets across the country published the latest case totals and measles-related deaths, in a kind of macabre box score. Less than a mile and a half from where paradegoers gathered stood Geisinger Lewistown Hospital, where an 18-year-old Amish girl had been reportedly treated for the measles before ultimately succumbing to a rare complication of the infection last month. She was the state’s fourth measles-related death. A fifth and sixth, in Lancaster and Franklin Counties, soon followed. Yet during a visit to Mifflin County earlier this month, and in conversations with area members of the Amish and non-Amish communities, there were few tangible signs of concern. No widespread panic. No state-held news conferences, like the one organized this summer in Lancaster County, to extoll the benefits of vaccination. At the epicenter of the state’s outbreak, measles seems to have been largely met with a sort of shrugging detachment. By locals outside of the Amish community, who largely see the outbreak as not their problem. By elected officials, who have been reluctant to address publicly the community’s growing public health crisis. Even by members of the Amish community themselves, who — hit disproportionately hard due to low rates of vaccination — have insisted that the virus is simply part of life. As cases here continue a troubling rise, there seems to be, for many, little sense in fretting. “My dad used to say, ‘Worry is from the devil,’” says Mose Yoder, a member of the local Amish community. “We still have to do what we have to do.” Mifflin County is home to an estimated 5,500 Amish people, a community long leery of modern advances, at times including healthcare and vaccines. Mifflin County’s Amish communities tend to be more conservative and traditional than those living in Lancaster County, which has the highest total case counts in the state. The county as a whole trends heavily Republican, a faction in which vaccine skepticism is higher . Seventh-grade measles vaccination rates in the county — which include both Amish and non-Amish students — sit at around 77%, among the lowest in the state. (Philadelphia County, by contrast, is at 97%.) It remains unclear whether — and how far — the outbreak has extended beyond the Amish community to the county’s other residents. But the outbreak has clearly spread widely among the area’s Amish. Vaccination efforts in Mifflin are likely complicated by the local Amish communities’ attitudes around technologies and interaction with outsiders, said Steven Nolt, professor of history and Anabaptist studies and director of the Young Center for Anabaptist and Pietist Studies at Elizabethtown College. “You can’t just say, ‘It’s the law of the land,’” says Helge Hartung, a physician at the Central Pennsylvania Clinic in Belleville, which treats a large number of Mifflin County Amish and has been working to vaccinate them. “That doesn’t work.” Since July, 193 cases have been confirmed in Mifflin County, with 76 new cases reported in the last two weeks — more new cases than any county in the state over that period. Both numbers, experts say, are almost certainly significant undercounts. Yet, during a visit to Mifflin County earlier this month, it seemed to largely be business as usual. At the Sharp Shopper grocery store in Belleville, both horse-drawn buggies and late-model SUVs still fill the parking lot. At the Wednesday livestock auction and farmers market, a heavily attended weekly event, men in straw hats and suspenders commingle with guys in pickup trucks and novelty T-shirts (“My wife says I only have two faults: Don’t listen and something else.”). In the small shops and restaurants of downtown Lewistown, patrons talk about the weather and the Eagles’ offense — but not the virus spreading rapidly through their county. “I haven’t heard one customer talk about it,” says Deb Guard, who owns the Lemon & Lime craft shop in downtown Lewistown. “Seriously, not one.” “I haven’t heard one customer talk about it. Seriously, not one.” Even the online rumblings that have been known to percolate here — politically infused back-and-forth about vaccine safety and efficacy — rarely spill into public, residents say. Over at radio station WMRF-FM 95.7, just off the town square, morning host Rocco Pallotto routinely wrangles a chatty stable of listeners. “You ask them [whether] they like apple, pumpkin spice, or caramel better — oh, boy ,” he says. But when Pallotto put out a call recently to discuss news of the local measles outbreak, he didn’t receive anything in response. “There’s just nothing there,” he says. Asked about the rising cases, Amanda Haines, a home health aide and mother of two vaccinated sons from Lewistown, shrugged last week as she sipped coffee outside the East End Coffee Co. As she put it: “I’m more worried about my kid [coming home with] lice.” In Belleville, home to many of Mifflin County’s Amish, it’s difficult to find anyone who hasn’t been touched by the current measles outbreak. It reached Bennie Peachey’s family not long ago, leaving two of his five children — the 9- and the 7-year-old — bedridden, covered “head to toe” in rash. The father himself has so far been spared — “unless I have it now,” said Peachey, a lithe man of 33, as he fussed with a tractor outside the family’s barn. If so, he noted, “It’s not too bad.” The Amish have looked out for each other. They know which neighboring farmhouses are grappling with the infection. They trade home remedies for mitigating the virus’ effects — blackberry juice and yarrow tea for hydration, sunglasses for a sensitivity to light. Some make a point not to bathe while ill, out of an unfounded fear it might push the virus deeper into the body. “We want to take care,” said one Amish woman who declined to give her name. “But we feel that God is in control.” A lack of information — both inside and outside the Amish community — has been an ongoing issue. At least one local Amish newspaper — Die Botschaft — decided not to publish the infection totals and has opted not to wade into coverage of the outbreak, including any recommendations regarding the vaccine. “We just don’t get into that stuff,“ says David Lapp, owner and editor of the Millersburg-based publication. ”It’s not really our focus, or our target.” A spokesperson for the Pennsylvania Department of Health said last week that its efforts to curb the spread of the disease in Mifflin County include distributing “educational materials and ... flyers about vaccine clinics.” The state is working with local healthcare providers to build trust on outreach and vaccination efforts. State Secretary of Health Debra Bogen recently traveled to Mifflin County to meet with local healthcare providers, the spokesperson added, to ensure they “have the tools they need to continue to provide educational information and MMR vaccinations to the affected community.” Local officials, however, have been reluctant to discuss a topic that has routinely devolved into politically infused rhetoric. “We don’t think that we can add much to your inquiry,” Robert Postal, one of three Mifflin County commissioners, said in an email. Some in Mifflin County have filled the void with rumor and innuendo, especially in the weeks following the 18-year-old’s death. The county coroner, a Republican, posted a news release on Facebook saying the young woman died of acute disseminated encephalomyelitis, a rare and serious neurological complication of measles. Yet the gossip that reached Randy Wilsoncroft, a local who runs a stand at the weekly Belleville auction and market, speculated erroneously the girl suffered from underlying health issues that had exacerbated her infection — even as her father described her, in a September interview with the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette, as “perfectly healthy.” “I heard she had West Nile and Lyme disease,” Wilsoncroft said. Back in medical school, when Katie Mascia learned about the measles, the lesson had the feeling of a long-ago relic. “It was almost like a historical lecture — like, ‘Oh, yeah, this is what measles looked like back in our grandparents’ age,’” said Mascia, now director of clinical services at the Central Pennsylvania Clinic in Belleville. “I never expected to see a measles case when I graduated from medical school, that’s for sure.” Ever since the first patients began showing up this summer with coughs and fever, she and other local healthcare workers now find themselves on the front lines of responding to an outbreak that has sickened more than 1,200 people statewide. “I never expected to see a measles case when I graduated from medical school, that’s for sure.” Though most people recover from measles without incident, nearly one in five infections reported in Pennsylvania has resulted in hospitalization. In the worst cases, patients arrive struggling to breathe, or must be life-flighted out of Lewistown to larger facilities across the state. Measles-positive mothers in the state have passed the virus on to their newborns, who are sometimes born with rashes and at risk for other severe diseases. Two Lancaster County infants with confirmed cases have died. In the sterile corridors of the county’s clinics and hospitals, patient stoicism can quickly turn to panic. “I see fear,” says Alexander Haines, an inpatient nursing manager at Geisinger Lewistown Hospital, which has treated 72 measles cases to date. “I see a lot of fear.” At the Central Pennsylvania Clinic, physicians have done their best to pierce an often staunch reluctance to a vaccine that has proved 97% effective at preventing the infection. The Amish people who spoke with The Inquirer this month shared a range of opinions that varied widely — sometimes within a single home. Some called the decision not to vaccinate a religious one. Some expressed concern that the side effects of the shots could be greater than the infection itself. Others have received one shot of the MMR vaccine, offering strong protection, but not a second, which experts recommend. “It’s a personal decision,” says Aaron B. Kanagy, a father of eight in Belleville, who contracted the measles as a child, which means he should now have immunity to further illness. Amid the current outbreak, vaccine resistance might be waning. In August and September alone, healthcare providers in Mifflin County administered nearly 800 MMR vaccinations, according to figures provided by the state — a significant jump from the 50 to 75 vaccinations typically administered each month. Last month, meanwhile, the Central Pennsylvania Clinic, which largely treats members of the local Amish and Mennonite communities, held a vaccination clinic locally. Originally, only 15 people signed up to be inoculated. By the time the event was held — just a few days after the death of the 18-year-old on Sept. 11 — 73 people arrived to receive a shot, Mascia said. Still, a wide swath of the area’s Amish — and many other residents — likely remain vulnerable to an airborne disease known to infect nine of 10 unvaccinated people exposed to it. Sitting inside the Belleville clinic, Mascia described the “heartbreak” of the ongoing spread, one that — with cold-and-flu season fast approaching — she fears could pose new challenges, including fewer available hospital beds. As she spoke, the clinic’s executive director appeared in the doorway. “A van with a big 6 on it just pulled in,” the woman reported. Mascia glanced out an office window, watched as an out-of-town TV news truck eased into a parking spot. “We’re very popular lately,” she said. On a vast plot of Belleville land, inside a simple farmhouse, is a small, dark bedroom where the Kanagy family keeps its ill. When the 12-year-old, Lizzie, first contracted the virus last month, the family — Aaron and Katie Kanagy and their eight children — did their best to keep their daughter quarantined even as they braced for a household barrage. By early October, the twins — Lomie and Lena, both 8 years old — hadn’t been feeling well, though they were still on their feet. At the height of the 12-year-old’s illness, Aaron Jr., 7, spent hours at his sister’s bedside, fetching her glasses of water and regaling her with tales from the school day. “I’m pretty much expecting it,” says Katie Kanagy. The Kanagys, like many of the area’s Amish, are old enough to remember the region’s last significant measles outbreak, in the late 1980s, when the infection roiled the Amish community and at least three of the 10 Amish schools across Lawrence and Mercer Counties were temporarily shuttered. But they made it through that OK, they insist. If the infection is coming, Aaron Kanagy said one recent afternoon, as he looked out over the family’s 63 acres of farmland, he would prefer it come soon. “Then,” he said, “it would be done.” While top officials publicly spar over whether Pennsylvanians are dying of measles, a midwife in Amish country believes the outbreak is so bad she wants to be able to carry the MMR vaccine herself. Chester County is the only Philadelphia region county where the virus is actively spreading. Local doctors say they’re worried that even more cases are spreading under the radar in under-vaccinated pockets of Lancaster County, where 31 cases have been detected. The outbreak has spread to more than half of Pennsylvania's 67 counties. Some families are deciding to vaccinate, while others are preparing to get sick. After measles swept through her household, the young woman wanted to get vaccinated. Her doctor said she couldn't because she had leukemia and the treatments had weakened her immune system. Thomas Jefferson University students will face off against each other this Saturday to see whose siege machine can launch pumpkins the furthest. The health department says the treatment is unproven, but studies in other cities have had promising results. The owner of the bar Kung Fu Necktie was not looking for love, and neither was his now-wife. The outbreak has been ongoing since July, with 76 new cases reported in the last two weeks. The publication date is 2026-10-18.
Location and population
Mifflin County, Pennsylvania, a rural enclave of 46,000 people, with an estimated 5,500 Amish residents. The outbreak has affected both Amish and non-Amish communities.
Event
A measles outbreak has been reported in Mifflin County, with 193 confirmed cases since July and 76 new cases in the last two weeks. The outbreak has resulted in at least four measles-related deaths, including an 18-year-old Amish girl who succumbed to a rare complication last month.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has spread widely among the Amish community, with cases reported in multiple households. The county has the highest infection rate in the state, and vaccination rates for seventh-grade students are among the lowest in the state, at 77%. The outbreak has caused significant public health concern, with experts noting that case numbers are likely undercounts.
Response, status and uncertainty
The outbreak is under investigation, with vaccination efforts being complicated by the Amish community’s attitudes toward modern technologies and interaction with outsiders. Public health officials have not held state-level news conferences, and there is limited public awareness or concern within the community. The county coroner reported the death of an Amish girl as acute disseminated encephalomyelitis, a rare complication of measles.

宾夕法尼亚州对先天性 麻疹 发出警报——这是美国几十年来从未见过的情况

Pennsylvania issues alert on congenital measles—something US hasn’t seen for decades
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚州卫生部门于 2026 年 10 月 19 日发布健康警报,强调孕妇 麻疹 病例和先天性 麻疹 病例激增,这是几十年来从未出现过的情况。该警报包括管理怀孕期间和新生儿期间 麻疹 暴露和病例的临床指南。截至2026年10月19日,该州今年报告了1,286例麻疹病例,其中247人住院,6人死亡等待调查。至少一例先天性 麻疹 病例导致新生儿死亡。麻疹病毒 可在子宫内或出生期间/出生后传播给胎儿,导致严重的多系统疾病和长期并发症,如免疫遗忘症和 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 (SSPE)。该部门正在按年龄追踪病例,并建议在六天内接触该病毒的孕妇静脉注射免疫球蛋白。他们还强调需要对受影响的新生儿的并发症进行长期随访和监测。暴发 与全国 疫苗接种 发病率下降有关,从而使病毒在疫苗接种不足的社区中迅速传播。

美国宾夕法尼亚州,42 个县报告了病例。受影响的人群包括孕妇和新生儿,特别是 4 岁以下的人群。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pennsylvania issues alert on congenital measles—something US hasn’t seen for decades

News Brief
Pennsylvania’s health department issued a health alert on October 19, 2026, highlighting a surge in measles cases among pregnant women and congenital measles, a condition not seen in decades. The alert includes clinical guidance for managing measles exposures and cases during pregnancy and in newborns. As of October 19, 2026, the state reported 1,286 measles cases this year, with 247 hospitalized and six deaths pending investigation. At least one congenital measles case led to a newborn death. The measles virus can be transmitted to a fetus in utero or during/after birth, causing severe multisystem disease and long-term complications like immune amnesia and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE). The department is tracking cases by age and recommends intravenous immunoglobulin for pregnant women exposed to the virus within six days. They also emphasize the need for long-term follow-up and monitoring for complications in affected newborns. The outbreak is linked to declining vaccination rates nationwide, allowing the virus to spread rapidly in under-vaccinated communities.
Original source text (English)
Pennsylvania’s health department issued a health alert Monday as cases of measles in pregnancy and, in turn, cases of congenital measles have continued to increase. Before the current outbreak—which continues to rage across the state—congenital measles cases hadn’t been seen in decades, the department noted in its advisory. The alert included detailed clinical guidance for managing measles exposures and cases during pregnancy as well as cases in newborns. Many doctors have never seen such cases in their careers given the decades of successful use of the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine. But now, with vaccination rates slipping nationwide, the highly infectious virus is better able to get footholds in communities and spread rapidly through under-vaccinated pockets, as is happening in Pennsylvania. As of October 19, Pennsylvania had reported 1,286 measles cases this year across 42 counties. Of those, 247 have been hospitalized, and six people have reportedly died (though the latest reported death is still pending an investigation by the state health department before it is added to the state tally). Ars Video How Scientists Respond to Science Deniers In a response to a request for comment, a spokesperson for Pennsylvania’s health department said officials have confirmed that 66 of the state’s measles cases were in pregnant women. The department does not keep a specific tally of congenital measles cases because the definition of these cases is not currently standardized. However, it noted that that it does track cases by age, and 157 cases have been recorded in children under the age of 4. At least one case of congenital measles led to a death; the first death reported in the current outbreak was in a newborn baby boy , who died immediately after birth while his mother was severely ill with measles. Risky infection Pregnant people who have not been vaccinated against measles nor previously had the infection are susceptible. A case of measles during pregnancy increases the risk of hospitalization, pneumonia, the need for oxygen support or mechanical ventilation, and death. It also increases the risk of miscarriage, stillbirth, low birth weight, and preterm labor. The MMR vaccine is not recommended during pregnancy because it is a live attenuated vaccine and poses a theoretical risk of causing an infection in a fetus. If a pregnant person is exposed to the virus, they’re recommended to receive intravenous immunoglobulin to help protect against infection—but only if it is given within six days of exposure. If there’s a measles infection during pregnancy, the measles virus can pass to a fetus in utero or can transmit during or just after birth, leading to a congenital case. These cases can vary widely in symptoms and severity. Some babies will remain asymptomatic while others develop severe, multisystem disease and death. Some common symptoms of congenital measles include fever and jaundice. They can also develop the telltale measles rash, but it is sometimes delayed or atypical given a newborn’s underdeveloped immune system. Other symptoms can include low platelet counts, pneumonia, and neurologic complications. Even if a newborn survives the acute infection, they are at risk of long-term complications. These include immune amnesia, a type of immunosuppression in which the measles virus destroys immune cells and adaptive responses. They are also at risk of subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), a progressive, fatal neurological disorder that can strike years after an infection. The alert warns that babies with congenital measles need long-term clinical follow-up and monitoring for these neurologic and immunologic complications, and parents need counseling on how to spot early signs of SSPE, which include seizures, regression in milestones, and academic difficulties in older children. This story was updated on October 20 to include case information from the Pennsylvania state health department. Beth Mole Senior Health Reporter Beth Mole Senior Health Reporter Beth is Ars Technica’s Senior Health Reporter. Beth has a Ph.D. in microbiology from the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill and attended the Science Communication program at the University of California, Santa Cruz. She specializes in covering infectious diseases, public health, and microbes. 150 Comments

Scale, impact and risk
As of October 19, 2026, Pennsylvania had reported 1,286 measles cases this year, with 247 hospitalized and six deaths pending investigation. At least one congenital measles case resulted in a newborn death. The measles virus can pass to a fetus in utero or transmit during or after birth, leading to congenital cases. These cases can result in severe, multisystem disease and death, as well as long-term complications like immune amnesia and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE).
Response, status and uncertainty
The health department is actively monitoring and providing clinical guidance for managing measles exposures and cases during pregnancy. They track cases by age and note that congenital measles cases are not standardized. The department is also advising on long-term follow-up and monitoring for complications in affected newborns.

Wisconsin still fourth-worst in country as measles cases surpass 300

News Brief
Wisconsin has reported over 300 measles cases in 2026, with outbreaks in eight counties. The latest data from the Wisconsin Department of Health Services show 308 cases, with the largest increase in Chippewa County, where the count more than doubled since last week. As of October 22, close to 4,600 measles cases have been reported in the U.S., making Wisconsin the fourth state with the most cases, behind Pennsylvania, South Carolina, and Utah. The outbreak is attributed to slipping vaccination coverage, which experts say is the primary cause. The measles vaccine is available through various channels, including doctors' offices, local health departments, pharmacies, and pop-up clinics, and is covered by most insurance plans. The U.S. is re-engaging with Myanmar's military-backed government, following talks in Tokyo two weeks ago. The country could lose its measles elimination status, which was granted in 2000, as the disease no longer circulates continuously. A World Health Organization commission is expected to vote on this decision during its November meeting in Washington, D.C. Residents are encouraged to check their vaccination status through the Wisconsin Immunization Registry and seek medical attention if they suspect measles.
Original source text (English)
Wisconsin has surpassed 300 measles cases in 2026 tied to outbreaks in eight counties. The latest data from the Wisconsin Department of Health Services show 308 cases from ongoing outbreaks that began in early July: The largest increase in measles cases reported Oct. 23 comes from Chippewa County, where the count more than doubled since last week.
