Wisconsin remains the state with the fourth-most cases, behind Pennsylvania, South Carolina and Utah. Experts across the health care field say there's one basic reason to blame: slipping measles vaccination coverage. The country could likely lose its measles elimination status, which was granted in 2000 and means the disease does not circulate continuously. A commission of the World Health Organization is expected to vote on the decision during its meeting in November in Washington, D.C. More: Wisconsin measles cases grow in counties with low vaccination rates More: Measles in Richland County as southwest Wisconsin outbreak keeps growing Measles symptoms typically start a week to two weeks after infection with a high fever, cough, runny nose and red, watery eyes. A few days after the first symptoms arise, red spots or bumps appear on the face and near the hairline. That rash will spread to the rest of the body. People may also get tiny white spots inside the mouth a few days after symptoms begin. If you think you may have measles, avoid others and call a doctor's office or clinic before showing up to receive medical attention. Wisconsin residents can check their measles, mumps and rubella vaccination status by visiting the Wisconsin Immunization Registry at dhfswir.org . The measles vaccine is covered by most insurance plans and is available at doctors' offices, local health departments, pharmacies and pop-up vaccine clinics.Those who need assistance affording vaccines can access them through the Vaccines for Children and Vaccines for Adults programs. US measles cases hit 4,588, more than twice last year’s total - CIDRAP

B routine priorityPublic-health news

US measles cases hit 4,588, more than twice last year’s total - CIDRAP

News Brief
The news report from CIDRAP, published on October 23, 2026, highlights a significant increase in measles cases in the United States, with a total of 4,588 cases reported, more than double the number from the previous year. Pennsylvania is identified as the primary hotspot, contributing nearly 30% of the national cases. The report notes that while the measles vaccine is less effective when given to infants at 6 months, it may still offer protection against severe complications. The situation is described as ongoing, with no specific mention of containment measures or further developments. The data is based on confirmed reports and official tracking, but no details on the source of the outbreak or specific prevention strategies are provided. The report emphasizes the need for continued vigilance and vaccination efforts to control the spread of measles.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

事实证明,美国的一名 麻疹 暴发 特别致命。这就是原因 - 斯克里普斯新闻

One US measles outbreak is proving particularly deadly. Here’s why - Scripps News
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚州正在进行的 麻疹 麻疹 麻疹 疫情已成为美国 30 多年来规模最大、死亡人数最多的疫情,约 1,300 例病例中已有 5 人死亡,其中 6 人正在接受审查。死亡率至少比历史平均水平高出1.5倍,专家认为这是由于受影响社区的独特脆弱性,特别是阿米什人和平原群体,其中未接种疫苗的成人和婴儿比例很高。CDC 已派出一名疾病侦探帮助评估 暴发 的规模和轨迹,但数据仍然过时,预计漏报。公共卫生官员担心 麻疹 的影响,尤其是对孕妇及其婴儿的影响,因为 麻疹 并发症对于未接种疫苗的成年人来说更为严重。麻疹 凸显了在一个之前被淘汰的国家中跟踪和响应 麻疹 的挑战,而 麻疹 死亡导致的政治紧张局势使了解全面情况的努力变得复杂。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年4月开始,最新的死亡和病例报告于2026年10月21日发布。目前死亡率至少比平均水平高1.5倍。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在美国宾夕法尼亚州,主要影响阿米什人和更广泛的平原社区,这些社区与医疗保健系统有着独特的互动方式。受影响的人群包括未接种疫苗的成人和婴儿。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州正在进行的 麻疹 暴发 已导致 5 人死亡,其中 6 人正在接受审查,报告病例总数约为 1,300 例。死亡率明显高于历史平均水平,暴发 被描述为美国 30 多年来规模最大、死亡人数最多的一次。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 导致死亡率比平均水平至少高出 1.5 倍,住院和死亡等严重后果更为常见。宾夕法尼亚州社区的独特脆弱性,包括大量未接种疫苗的成人和婴儿,导致了风险的增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 已派出一名疾病侦探前往宾夕法尼亚州,以更好地了解 暴发 的规模和轨迹。然而,该数据被认为是过时的,并且预计会漏报,因此很难估计 暴发 的真实范围。公共卫生专家正在努力加深对情况的了解。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

One US measles outbreak is proving particularly deadly. Here’s why - Scripps News

News Brief
An ongoing measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has become the largest and deadliest in the US in over 30 years, with five deaths and a sixth under review among about 1,300 cases. The death rate is at least 1.5 times higher than historical averages, and experts suggest this is due to the unique vulnerabilities of the affected community, particularly the Amish and Plain groups, which have a high proportion of unvaccinated adults and infants. The CDC has sent a disease detective to help assess the outbreak's scale and trajectory, but the data remains outdated, and underreporting is expected. Public health officials are concerned about the impact of the outbreak, especially on pregnant women and their babies, as measles complications are more severe for unvaccinated adults. The outbreak highlights the challenges of tracking and responding to measles in a country where it was previously eliminated, and the political tensions over measles deaths complicate efforts to understand the full scope of the situation.
Original source text (English)

Here’s why With five deaths and a sixth under review out of about 1,300 cases, Pennsylvania's measles death rate is running well above historical averages, experts say. Prev Next Pennsylvania's measles outbreak is the largest, deadliest in the US in 30+ years, with 5 deaths among about 1,300 cases and a death rate above average. (Scripps News) By: Deidre McPhillips Posted 5:53 PM, Oct 21, 2026 Share Copy linkFacebookNextdoorXThreadsRedditLinkedInEmailWhatsAppMessengerPrint Add Scripps News as a Google Preferred Source An ongoing measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has quickly become the largest and deadliest in the United States in more than 30 years. Historical data suggests that 1 to 3 people will die out of every 1,000 unvaccinated people who contract measles. But Pennsylvania has reported five measles-associated deaths — with a sixth under review — out of about 1,300 total reported cases since the start of the outbreak in April. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE This puts the measles death rate in Pennsylvania at least 1.5 times higher than average — and possibly up to 5 times higher. RELATED STORY | Sixth measles-related death reported as Pennsylvania battles widespread outbreak It’s not likely that the measles virus has changed. One factor might be the size of the outbreak and a probable undercount of cases: It’s difficult to know how many people have been affected. It may also be the unique vulnerabilities of the Pennsylvania community where measles is raging. “There clearly is a shift — not in the biology but a shift in the epidemiology that we’re seeing here in Pennsylvania,” said Dr. Matthew Ferrari, director of the Center for Infectious Disease Dynamics at Penn State University. Experts say the outbreak is even larger than official data suggests, and it’s not clear how big it is. The US successfully eliminated measles decades ago, making it difficult to estimate how bad the current resurgence has gotten. A “disease detective” from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention arrived in Pennsylvania this week to help better understand the true scale of the outbreak and its trajectory, the state health department said. The scientist, with the CDC’s Epi-Aid program, is also tasked with evaluating the impact of local vaccination efforts. Scientists and public health workers typically estimate current disease trends by using patterns from recent years, scaling changes against a few variables like birth rates and vaccination rates. But measles data in the US is quite “outdated,” said Dr. Remy Pasco, a research associate at the University of Colorado Boulder. “It’s not endemic, so we don’t have as much data on it. And because the data has been so scarce, anything we can estimate is with a big grain of salt.” IN CASE YOU MISSED IT | Measles surge triggers state of emergency in New York Pasco has worked on an analysis to estimate the scale of last year’s measles outbreak in Texas. Some underreporting of cases is expected, he says, but it’s unlikely that the rate of underreporting would be the same — even when comparing recent outbreaks in different parts of the US. There are some “peculiarities” with the outbreak in Pennsylvania, he said. Hospitalization rates with this outbreak are higher than expected. And the Amish and broader Plain community – so named for their simple way of dressing – has a unique way of interacting with the health care system. Severe outcomes, such as hospitalizations and deaths, are generally the best benchmark for understanding how widespread a measles outbreak is, public health experts say. Not every person who is infected with measles will seek care or testing, but severe cases are more visible in the health care system, and tracking them can help set the ratio to understand the full scale. A uniquely susceptible group One signal that this outbreak is different: A disproportionate share of cases are among adults. About two-thirds of measles cases in Pennsylvania have been among adults, and all but a few were unvaccinated. Nationwide, however, cases skew younger; CDC data shows that more than 60% of cases in the US over the past two years have been among children or teens. The large population of unvaccinated adults in Pennsylvania, particularly among Amish and other members of the Plain community, has created a uniquely risky outbreak situation, Ferrari said. Measles complications tend to be more severe for unvaccinated adults than for unvaccinated children, he said. But the low rates of protection among adults in the community also mean vulnerable infants are more likely to be born without protective maternal antibodies. “We normally think about measles as being a childhood infection,” Ferrari said. “Most people have been exposed to measles or have been vaccinated against measles by the time they reach reproductive age.” That’s true around the world. Countries typically have high vaccination rates or endemic measles spread, which means people are exposed when they’re younger. Much of the affected population in Pennsylvania falls into a uniquely susceptible group: both unvaccinated and going decades without exposure because the US had so few cases for so long. If there’s any explanation for a higher death rate than expected in Pennsylvania, Ferrari said, this would be it: Antibodies — whether from vaccination or infection — aren’t being passed on from women of reproductive age to their babies. Two infants who were infected with measles have died in Pennsylvania this year, both too young to receive the measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine. “Those are absolutely deaths that wouldn’t have been occurring elsewhere in the world,” Ferrari said. The Pennsylvania Department of Health published an advisory Monday with guidance for measles in pregnancy and congenital measles. “Due to the ongoing measles outbreak, there is an increase in measles cases during pregnancy, along with rising concerns about congenital measles, which has been identified for the first time in decades,” the advisory said. Public health knowledge and guidance on this topic continues to develop, the health department said, but a measles infection increases the risk of complications for pregnant mothers and their babies. ‘Boots on the ground epidemiology’ Another complication to understanding this outbreak is an unusually political fight over measles deaths. Pennsylvania leaders have clashed with federal leaders over measles deaths in Pennsylvania, putting a key data point for understanding the scale of the outbreak into question. Although the state health department has officially reported five measles-associated deaths, the CDC dashboard includes only three — noting that the “number is subject to change” as additional information becomes available and that the agency is working to “develop a standardized case definition for deaths due to measles.” In September, the Pennsylvania health department “began the formal process of requesting an Epi-Aid” from the CDC, but Dr. Debra Bogen, the department secretary, noted in a letter to CDC Director Dr. Erica Schwartz that the request would be rescinded “if CDC is unable to transparently and accurately communicate information about those measles-associated deaths.” Last week, Bogen sent another letter to Schwartz thanking her for the “support and technical assistance from CDC professional staff throughout this outbreak response” and accepting the Epi-Aid as the state health department and CDC staff “continue to collaborate effectively” on measles outbreak response work. “While the Department is prepared to accept this Epi-Aid, I again ask the CDC to acknowledge the loss of five Pennsylvanians to measles and to report these deaths on the CDC’s public dashboard so that we can repair and rebuild trust in the public health system in the U.S.,” Bogen wrote. “By not acknowledging the deaths, the CDC obscures the seriousness of this disease and the toll of measles across the U.S.” Without the historical data to use as a benchmark, understanding the true scale of the measles outbreak in Pennsylvania will require investigative public health work. It’s “classic boots on the ground epidemiology,” Ferrari said, with epidemiologists out and about in the community asking questions about recent health history and vaccination coverage. “These are outbreaks in populations that haven’t seen this in a long time, and so we can’t rely on the standard approaches that are predicated on consistent or persistent transmission.” The-CNN-WireTM & © 2026 Cable News Network, Inc., a Warner Bros. Discovery Company. All rights reserved. 麻疹 更新:2026 年 10 月 23 日

Measles Update: October 23, 2026
新闻简报这篇发表于 2026 年 10 月 23 日的文章报道称,美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 记录的 麻疹 病例每周增加 4.0%,使 2026 年美国确诊病例总数达到 4588 例。与 2025 年的 2289 起案件总数相比,这标志着显着上升,2026 年的数字大约翻了一番。其中 96% 的病例与 暴发 相关,报告有 479 例住院治疗,占所有病例的 10%。CDC 指出,96% 的患者未接种疫苗或 疫苗接种 状态未知,这凸显了免疫接种率的下降。该机构还致力于标准化 麻疹 死亡病例定义。文章虽然提到了宾夕法尼亚州第六起与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件,但强调该数据仅包括确诊病例,可能存在疑似病例。CDC 继续监测 暴发,并与州流行病学家合作追踪该病毒的传播和影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年10月23日。活动日期为2026年10月22日,2026年10月23日报告的病例数环比增加。
地点与对象
麻疹 案件遍布美国 47 个司法管辖区,其中包括宾夕法尼亚州等州。受影响人群包括5岁以下儿童、20岁及以上成人以及5至19岁人群,其中27例年龄不明。
事件
CDC 报告称,麻疹 病例环比增加 4.0%,使 2026 年美国确诊病例总数达到 4588 例。这是美国正在进行的 麻疹 麻疹 的更新,2026 年报告了 3 例 麻疹 死亡。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月22日,美国有4588例确诊的麻疹病例,其中96%的病例与麻疹相关。CDC 报告有 479 例住院病例,占病例的 10%,其中 5 岁以下儿童 (16%) 和 20 岁及以上成人 (14%) 的住院率较高。2026 年的总数大约是 2025 年 2289 例总数的两倍。此外,国际游客中报告了 18 例 麻疹 病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在积极监测和报告 麻疹 病例和 麻疹 病例,重点是制定 麻疹 死亡的标准化病例定义。该数据仅包括确诊病例,疾控中心承认可能存在疑似病例,但未包含在当前统计数据中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles Update: October 23, 2026

News Brief
The article, published on October 23, 2026, reports that the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has documented a 4.0% week-over-week increase in measles cases, bringing the 2026 US total to 4588 confirmed cases. This marks a significant rise compared to the 2025 total of 2289 cases, with the 2026 figure being roughly double. Ninety-six percent of these cases are outbreak-associated, and 479 hospitalizations were reported, representing 10% of all cases. The CDC notes that 96% of patients were unvaccinated or had unknown vaccination status, highlighting a decline in immunization rates. The agency is also working on standardizing the case definition for measles deaths. While the article mentions a sixth measles-related death in Pennsylvania, it emphasizes that the data only includes confirmed cases, and probable cases may exist. The CDC continues to monitor the outbreak and is collaborating with state epidemiologists to track the spread and impact of the virus.

Ninety-six percent of cases are outbreak-associated. Advertisement Today, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) reported there were 4588* cases of measles in the US as of October 22, 2026. Week-over-week, the agency added 178 cases, a 4.0% increase. Last week, the federal agency reported there were 4410* confirmed cases.1,2 The increase is smaller than last week's 8.1% rise, when CDC added 330 cases.2 The 2026 total is now roughly double the 2289 cases reported for all of 2025.1 CDC said there were 4570 measles cases reported by 47 jurisdictions: Alabama, Alaska, Arizona, California, Colorado, Connecticut, Delaware, District of Columbia, Florida, Georgia, Idaho, Illinois, Indiana, Iowa, Kansas, Kentucky, Louisiana, Maine, Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, Minnesota, Missouri, Montana, Nebraska, New Jersey, New Mexico, New York City, New York State, North Carolina, North Dakota, Ohio, Oklahoma, Oregon, Pennsylvania, Rhode Island, South Carolina, South Dakota, Tennessee, Texas, Utah, Vermont, Virginia, Washington, West Virginia, Wisconsin, and Wyoming. A total of 18 measles cases were reported among international visitors to the United States.1 The federal agency reports there have been 43 new outbreaks** reported in 2026, and 96% of confirmed cases (4404 of 4588) are outbreak-associated (3020 from outbreaks starting in 2026 and 1384 from outbreaks starting in 2025).1 One new outbreak was added since last week.2 Hospitalizations and Age Distribution in 2026 Measles Cases CDC reported 479 hospitalizations among the 4588 cases, or 10%.1 Last week, the agency reported 451 hospitalizations, which means 28 more patients were hospitalized week-over-week.2 Hospitalization rates varied by age. Children younger than 5 years had a rate of 16% (119 of 766), and adults 20 years and older had a rate of 14% (268 of 1881).1 Those aged 5 to 19 years had a rate of 5% (91 of 1914).1 People younger than 20 years account for 58% of cases (2680 of 4588).1 Adults 20 years and older make up 41% (1881 cases), and the age of 27 cases is unknown.1 Vaccination Status and Coverage Trends Ninety-six percent of 2026 patients were unvaccinated or had unknown vaccination status, while 2% had received 1 MMR dose and 2% had received 2 doses.1 CDC lists 3 measles deaths in 2026, a number the agency says is subject to change as reviews are completed.1 Frequently Asked Questions What is measles? Measles is a highly contagious viral disease caused by the measles virus, transmitted through respiratory droplets and airborne particles. It was declared eliminated in the US in 2000 but has resurged due to declining vaccination rates. What are the symptoms of measles? Symptoms include high fever, cough, runny nose, red eyes, and a characteristic red rash spreading from the face downward, typically appearing 3 to 5 days after fever onset. How is measles prevented? The MMR (measles, mumps, and rubella) vaccine is highly effective, with 2 doses providing approximately 97% protection. The CDC recommends children receive the first dose at 12 to 15 months and the second at 4 to 6 years. Kindergarten MMR coverage has fallen from 95.2% in the 2019-2020 school year to 92.4% in 2025-2026, leaving approximately 280,000 kindergartners at risk.1 CDC is also working with the Council of State and Territorial Epidemiologists to develop a standardized case definition for measles deaths.1 *It is important to note that the CDC says on its website that the federal agency is aware of probable measles cases being reported by jurisdictions. However, the data on this page only include confirmed cases that jurisdictions notified the CDC about. Therefore, there could be higher numbers in the US overall. **CDC reports the cumulative number of measles outbreaks (defined as 3 or more related cases) that have occurred this year in the US; states have the most up-to-date information about cases and outbreaks in their jurisdictions. View five news elements
Time
The article was published on October 23, 2026. The event date is October 22, 2026, with a week-over-week increase in cases reported on October 23, 2026.
Location and population
The measles cases are reported across 47 jurisdictions in the United States, including states like Pennsylvania. The affected population includes children younger than 5 years, adults 20 years and older, and people aged 5 to 19 years, with 27 cases having unknown ages.
Event
The CDC reported a 4.0% week-over-week increase in measles cases, bringing the 2026 US total to 4588 confirmed cases. This is an update on the ongoing measles outbreak in the US, with 3 measles deaths reported in 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
There were 4588 confirmed measles cases in the US as of October 22, 2026, with 96% of cases being outbreak-associated. The CDC reported 479 hospitalizations, or 10% of cases, with higher rates among children under 5 (16%) and adults 20 years and older (14%). The 2026 total is roughly double the 2025 total of 2289 cases. Additionally, 18 measles cases were reported among international visitors.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC is actively monitoring and reporting on measles cases and outbreaks, with a focus on developing a standardized case definition for measles deaths. The data includes only confirmed cases, and the CDC acknowledges that probable cases may exist but are not included in the current statistics.

新闻简报密歇根州德尔塔县已确认三起 麻疹 病例,这是自 1989 年以来首次发生。当地卫生部门正在与密歇根州卫生与公众服务部合作,调查这些病例并通知可能接触过的个人。麻疹 是一种高度传染性的空气传播疾病,症状包括高烧、皮疹、咳嗽、眼睛发红等。它可能导致严重的并发症,包括住院、失明、脑炎或死亡。CDC 已更新其政策,要求通过正式死亡证明来核实与 麻疹 相关的死亡。2026 年,这种疾病在美国呈上升趋势,全国报告病例超过 4,000 例。麻疹 疫苗 与流行性腮腺炎和风疹 (MMR) 相结合,可有效预防该疾病,两剂剂量可为大多数健康个体提供约 97% 的保护。然而,未接种疫苗的患者约占观察到的病例的70%,凸显了疫苗接种在控制暴发方面的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Three measles cases in Delta — first since 1989 - Dailypress.net

News Brief
Three measles cases have been confirmed in Delta County, Michigan, marking the first occurrence since 1989. The local health department, in collaboration with the Michigan Department of Health and Human Services, is investigating the cases and notifying individuals who may have been exposed. Measles is a highly contagious airborne disease with symptoms such as high fever, rash, cough, and red eyes. It can lead to severe complications, including hospitalization, blindness, encephalitis, or death. The CDC has updated its policy to require verification of measles-related deaths through formal death certificates. The disease has been rising in the U.S. in 2026, with over 4,000 cases reported nationwide. The measles vaccine, combined with mumps and rubella (MMR), is effective in preventing the disease, with two doses providing about 97% protection for most healthy individuals. However, unvaccinated patients account for about 70% of observed cases, highlighting the importance of vaccination in controlling outbreaks.
Original source text (English)

The state is the source of about a quarter of this year's measles cases in the U.S. (AP Photo/Matt Rourke) 1 / 4 Trending 1 Illinois man arrested for $665K scam of Escanaba man 2 MMR opens doors 3 Teen pleads no contest in drowning death 4 BraveBots fate uncertain 5 Three measles cases in Delta — first since 1989 6 Registry changes bring Halloween safety into focus 7 Mike in Gladstone view more DELTA COUNTY -- Three cases of measles have been confirmed in Delta County, announced Public Health of Delta & Menominee Counties (PHDM) on Wednesday afternoon. “The health department is working with the Michigan Department of Health and Human Services (MDHHS) to investigate the cases and notify anyone who may be exposed,” said PHDM in the release. The last time measles appeared in the two-county jurisdiction was 1989. These are the first cases in the Upper Peninsula in 2026. The highly-contagious airborne disease’s common symptoms are high fever, rash, cough and red eyes. The severity and danger, however, is more extreme than most other illnesses; one out of five people who catch it end up hospitalized, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). Complications range from effects as mild as an ear infection to as serious as blindness, encephalitis or death. “(Measles) can make you severely ill and lead to life-threatening complications like brain inflammation and pneumonia. It can also make you more likely to get sick with other infections,” says Cleveland Clinic. Before a vaccine was developed in 1963, almost every child in the United States contracted measles before the age of 15. An annual four million cases led to about 500 deaths and 48,000 hospitalizations every year in the U.S., reports the Infectious Diseases Society of America. The measles vaccine was combined with the vaccines for mumps and rubella in 1971; since then, infections fell dramatically, and a two-dose series became common practice. The CDC declared measles eliminated from the United States in 2000 thanks to high coverage with the MMR shots. However, with a noted drop in immunizations since 2019, the disease has been showing up more and more, with a massive surge this year. Ten years ago, the U.S. recorded just 70 confirmed cases of measles in 2016. The country counted 285 in 2024, and then a whopping 2,289 in 2025. In 2026, there have been over 4,000 measles cases. One case can generate up to 18 secondary infections where immunity is low, estimates the World Health Organization. “Measles is highly contagious, which means it spreads easily from person to person. Experts estimate that if one person in a room of unvaccinated people has measles, 9 out of 10 people in the room will catch it,” wrote Cleveland Clinic. “You're contagious about four days before you develop a rash until about four days after the rash starts.” The largest outbreaks in 2026 have been in Pennsylvania, where over 1,300 cases have been confirmed. The State of Pennsylvania states that five individuals have died from measles this year. At the federal level, however, officially only three deaths of 2026 are yet attributed to measles. As part of a directive from U.S. Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr., the CDC recently updated its policy to require that measles-related deaths be verified by the National Center for Health Statistics (NCHS) based on formal death certificates. Regardless, the fact remains that instances of measles -- known to be both very contagious and potentially deadly -- have been rising. In American residents between the years of 2001 and 2015, 1,789 measles cases were reported. That means the number of cases in one year (over 4,600 in 2026) amount to more that double the total seen over a cumulative 14-year span. About 70 percent of observed cases occur in unvaccinated patients. While symptoms may be treated, measles does not have a cure. However, as prevention is far more likely with immunization than without, medical professionals advise that people who can get the vaccine do. The MMR vaccine is live-attenuated, meaning that it contains weakened versions of the viruses that cause measles, mumps and rubella. For most healthy people, two injections four weeks apart provide about 97% protection. Some people, however, cannot tolerate the vaccine; people who are pregnant and those with weakened immune systems are advised to avoid attenuated viruses. There is another option: babies under 12 months, pregnant people and those with weak immune systems may be able to get a different treatment called immunoglobulin (IG). Primarily, though, those people’s health relies on “herd immunity” -- a term for when a population doesn’t see much sickness because a majority of individuals are protected and not spreading contagions. Getting the MMR shot or IG within 72 hours of exposure may stop a person from getting sick. With the measles virus known to be in the community, PHDM recommends some steps: – Check your vaccination status. “You can ask your doctor or contact the local health department,” says PHDM, but notes that people are likely protected if: (a) they have had at least one MMR vaccine, preferably two; (b) they have a record of immunity to measles, such as documented history of a past measles infection; or (c) were born before 1957. PHDM offers MMR vaccines. To make an appointment or ask questions, call PHDM at (906) 786-4111. Most insurance plans cover the vaccine. People may qualify for free or low-costs shots. Talk to your local health department or healthcare provider. – Watch for symptoms. Measles symptoms are high fever, which can reach over 104 degrees; cough; runny nose; red, watery eyes; tiny white spots in the mouth; a red, raised rash that starts on the face and spreads to the body. Symptoms generally show up one to two weeks after exposure but can take up to three weeks to present. – If you get symptoms, call a healthcare provider. The recommendation is to call before seeking medical help in order to avoid spreading the virus. 2 articles remaining... Starting at $4.00/week. 事实证明,美国的一名 麻疹 暴发 特别致命。这就是原因 - CNN

One US measles outbreak is proving particularly deadly. Here’s why - CNN
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚州正在进行的 麻疹 麻疹 事件已成为美国 30 多年来规模最大、死亡人数最多的事件,已报告了 5 例与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件,还有 6 例正在接受审查。自 2026 年 4 月开始的 暴发 已影响约 1,300 人,导致死亡率比平均水平至少高出 1.5 倍。这是由于阿米什人和其他平原社区独特的脆弱性,其中很大一部分病例是未接种疫苗的成年人和缺乏母体抗体的婴儿。CDC 已部署一名疾病侦探来评估 暴发 的规模和轨迹,以及当地 疫苗接种 工作的影响。公共卫生专家指出,这些数据已经过时且报告不足,因此很难准确衡量 暴发 的全部范围。暴发 强调了在先前消除该疾病的国家中估计 麻疹 趋势的挑战,以及与特定人群中低 疫苗接种 率相关的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 4 月开始,死亡报告于 2026 年 10 月。CDC 科学家于 2026 年 10 月抵达宾夕法尼亚州。
地点与对象
宾夕法尼亚州,特别是米夫林县,重点关注阿米什人和其他平原社区。受影响的人群包括未接种疫苗的成人和婴儿。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州正在进行的 麻疹 麻疹 事件已导致 5 例与 麻疹 相关的死亡,其中第 6 例正在接受审查。暴发是美国30多年来规模最大、死亡人数最多的一次,死亡率比平均水平至少高1.5倍。
规模、影响与风险
自 2026 年 4 月以来,宾夕法尼亚州报告了约 1,300 例 麻疹 病例,其中 5 例死亡,1 例正在接受审查。住院率高于预期,并且由于未接种疫苗的成人比例很高且婴儿缺乏母源抗体,暴发 被认为具有独特的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 已派出一名疾病侦探前往宾夕法尼亚州评估 暴发 及其轨迹。公共卫生专家正在使用住院和死亡等严重后果来估计 暴发 的规模,但这些数据被认为已经过时且报告不足。暴发 的独特特征,包括平原社区的 疫苗接种 怀疑态度,正在接受调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

One US measles outbreak is proving particularly deadly. Here’s why - CNN

News Brief
An ongoing measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has become the largest and deadliest in the United States in over 30 years, with five measles-associated deaths reported and a sixth under review. The outbreak, which began in April 2026, has affected approximately 1,300 individuals, resulting in a death rate at least 1.5 times higher than the average. This is attributed to the unique vulnerabilities of the Amish and other Plain communities, where a high proportion of cases are among unvaccinated adults and infants lacking maternal antibodies. The CDC has deployed a disease detective to assess the outbreak's scale and trajectory, as well as the impact of local vaccination efforts. Public health experts note that the data is outdated and underreported, making it difficult to accurately gauge the outbreak's full extent. The outbreak highlights the challenges of estimating measles trends in a country where the disease was previously eliminated, and the risks associated with low vaccination rates in specific populations.
Original source text (English)

US measles outbreak is proving particularly deadly. Here’s why Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! Two infants who were infected with measles have died in Pennsylvania this year, both too young to receive the measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine. Hannah Beier/Reuters An ongoing measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has quickly become the largest and deadliest in the United States in more than 30 years. Historical data suggests that 1 to 3 people will die out of every 1,000 unvaccinated people who contract measles. But Pennsylvania has reported five measles-associated deaths — with a sixth under review — out of about 1,300 total reported cases since the start of the outbreak in April. This puts the measles death rate in Pennsylvania at least 1.5 times higher than average — and possibly up to 5 times higher. Ad Feedback It’s not likely that the measles virus has changed. One factor might be the size of the outbreak and a probable undercount of cases: It’s difficult to know how many people have been affected. It may also be the unique vulnerabilities of the Pennsylvania community where measles is raging. “There clearly is a shift — not in the biology but a shift in the epidemiology that we’re seeing here in Pennsylvania,” said Dr. Matthew Ferrari, director of the Center for Infectious Disease Dynamics at Penn State University. Experts say the outbreak is even larger than official data suggests, and it’s not clear how big it is. The US successfully eliminated measles decades ago, making it difficult to estimate how bad the current resurgence has gotten. A “disease detective” from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention arrived in Pennsylvania this week to help better understand the true scale of the outbreak and its trajectory, the state health department said. The scientist, with the CDC’s Epi-Aid program, is also tasked with evaluating the impact of local vaccination efforts. Scientists and public health workers typically estimate current disease trends by using patterns from recent years, scaling changes against a few variables like birth rates and vaccination rates. But measles data in the US is quite “outdated,” said Dr. Remy Pasco, a research associate at the University of Colorado Boulder. “It’s not endemic, so we don’t have as much data on it. And because the data has been so scarce, anything we can estimate is with a big grain of salt.” Pasco has worked on an analysis to estimate the scale of last year’s measles outbreak in Texas. Some underreporting of cases is expected, he says, but it’s unlikely that the rate of underreporting would be the same — even when comparing recent outbreaks in different parts of the US. There are some “peculiarities” with the outbreak in Pennsylvania, he said. Hospitalization rates with this outbreak are higher than expected. And the Amish and broader Plain community – so named for their simple way of dressing – has a unique way of interacting with the health care system. Severe outcomes, such as hospitalizations and deaths, are generally the best benchmark for understanding how widespread a measles outbreak is, public health experts say. Not every person who is infected with measles will seek care or testing, but severe cases are more visible in the health care system, and tracking them can help set the ratio to understand the full scale. A uniquely susceptible group One signal that this outbreak is different: A disproportionate share of cases are among adults. About two-thirds of measles cases in Pennsylvania have been among adults, and all but a few were unvaccinated. Nationwide, however, cases skew younger; CDC data shows that more than 60% of cases in the US over the past two years have been among children or teens. The large population of unvaccinated adults in Pennsylvania, particularly among Amish and other members of the Plain community, has created a uniquely risky outbreak situation, Ferrari said. Measles complications tend to be more severe for unvaccinated adults than for unvaccinated children, he said. But the low rates of protection among adults in the community also mean vulnerable infants are more likely to be born without protective maternal antibodies. “We normally think about measles as being a childhood infection,” Ferrari said. “Most people have been exposed to measles or have been vaccinated against measles by the time they reach reproductive age.” That’s true around the world. Countries typically have high vaccination rates or endemic measles spread, which means people are exposed when they’re younger. Much of the affected population in Pennsylvania falls into a uniquely susceptible group: both unvaccinated and going decades without exposure because the US had so few cases for so long. Related article The exterior of Central Pennsylvania Clinic is seen in Belleville, Pennsylvania on Friday, October 2, 2026. The clinic specializes in genetic diseases and mutations specific to the Plains community, such as the Amish, Mennonites, Hutterites, and other closed religious communities. Mifflin County, where Belleville is situated, is becoming the center of the measles outbreak in the United States, particularly within the Plains community, as the communities are skeptical of vaccines. Michelle Gustafson for CNN Amid worst measles outbreak in decades, one clinic leans on years of hard-won trust to keep vulnerable people safe 13 min read If there’s any explanation for a higher death rate than expected in Pennsylvania, Ferrari said, this would be it: Antibodies — whether from vaccination or infection — aren’t being passed on from women of reproductive age to their babies. Two infants who were infected with measles have died in Pennsylvania this year, both too young to receive the measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine. “Those are absolutely deaths that wouldn’t have been occurring elsewhere in the world,” Ferrari said. The Pennsylvania Department of Health published an advisory Monday with guidance for measles in pregnancy and congenital measles. “Due to the ongoing measles outbreak, there is an increase in measles cases during pregnancy, along with rising concerns about congenital measles, which has been identified for the first time in decades,” the advisory said. Public health knowledge and guidance on this topic continues to develop, the health department said, but a measles infection increases the risk of complications for pregnant mothers and their babies. ‘Boots on the ground epidemiology’ Another complication to understanding this outbreak is an unusually political fight over measles deaths. Pennsylvania leaders have clashed with federal leaders over measles deaths in Pennsylvania, putting a key data point for understanding the scale of the outbreak into question. Although the state health department has officially reported five measles-associated deaths, the CDC dashboard includes only three — noting that the “number is subject to change” as additional information becomes available and that the agency is working to “develop a standardized case definition for deaths due to measles.” In September, the Pennsylvania health department “began the formal process of requesting an Epi-Aid” from the CDC, but Dr. Debra Bogen, the department secretary, noted in a letter to CDC Director Dr. Erica Schwartz that the request would be rescinded “if CDC is unable to transparently and accurately communicate information about those measles-associated deaths.” In June, Pennsylvania Secretary of Health Dr. Debra Bogen gives an update on the state’s response to measles cases in the Lancaster–Lebanon region. From Pennsylvania Department of Health/Facebook Last week, Bogen sent another letter to Schwartz thanking her for the “support and technical assistance from CDC professional staff throughout this outbreak response” and accepting the Epi-Aid as the state health department and CDC staff “continue to collaborate effectively” on measles outbreak response work. “While the Department is prepared to accept this Epi-Aid, I again ask the CDC to acknowledge the loss of five Pennsylvanians to measles and to report these deaths on the CDC’s public dashboard so that we can repair and rebuild trust in the public health system in the U.S.,” Bogen wrote. “By not acknowledging the deaths, the CDC obscures the seriousness of this disease and the toll of measles across the U.S.” Without the historical data to use as a benchmark, understanding the true scale of the measles outbreak in Pennsylvania will require investigative public health work. It’s “classic boots on the ground epidemiology,” Ferrari said, with epidemiologists out and about in the community asking questions about recent health history and vaccination coverage. “These are outbreaks in populations that haven’t seen this in a long time, and so we can’t rely on the standard approaches that are predicated on consistent or persistent transmission.” Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! 宾夕法尼亚州警告先天性 麻疹 和怀孕期间感染的增加 - NBC 新闻

Pennsylvania warns of rise in congenital measles and infection during pregnancy - NBC News
新闻简报2026年10月20日,宾夕法尼亚州卫生部门发布了一则警报,警告孕妇中麻疹感染病例增加,以及先天性麻疹病例的上升。自4月暴发暴发以来,该州已诊断出66例孕妇感染麻疹。卫生部门指出,妊娠期间感染麻疹会增加流产、早产和低出生体重的风险。据报告,至少有6名婴儿出生时患有先天性麻疹,其中2名出现肺炎并需要重症监护。先天性麻疹可能导致严重的长期并发症,包括亚急性硬化性全脑炎(亚急性硬化性全脑炎),该病几乎总是致命的。卫生部门强调,目前尚无针对先天性麻疹的特异性治疗,预防措施应集中在妊娠前的疫苗接种。然而,州法律限制披露妊娠结果,且暴发主要局限于阿米什家庭,这给数据收集和透明度带来了复杂性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 20 日星期一发布了关于孕妇 麻疹 感染增加的警报。截至 2026 年 10 月 20 日星期四,美国已有 4,410 例 麻疹 病例,截至 2026 年 10 月 20 日星期一,宾夕法尼亚州有 1,286 例。
地点与对象
宾夕法尼亚州,专门针对孕妇及其新生儿。暴发 在很大程度上仅限于可能并不总是寻求传统医疗援助的阿米什家庭。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部报告称,孕妇中的 麻疹 感染有所增加,并且先天性 麻疹 病例的增加令人担忧。该部门警告说,怀孕期间服用麻疹会增加流产、早产和低出生体重的风险。
规模、影响与风险
自 4 月份 麻疹 开始以来,宾夕法尼亚州已有 66 名孕妇被诊断出患有 麻疹。据传闻,至少有六名婴儿出生时患有先天性 麻疹,其中至少两名婴儿出现肺炎并被送入重症监护室。卫生部门指出,先天性 麻疹 可能致命,并可能在以后的生活中导致严重的大脑和免疫系统问题,包括几乎总是致命的 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 (SSPE)。
应对、状态与不确定性
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部表示,州法律禁止公布妊娠结果的详细信息,并且没有追踪病例的国家数据库。他们强调了怀孕前注射 疫苗接种 的重要性,并提到可以在接触后六天内给未接种疫苗的孕妇注射免疫球蛋白。该部门还强调,暴发 很大程度上仅限于阿米什家庭,这可能会影响数据的准确性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pennsylvania warns of rise in congenital measles and infection during pregnancy - NBC News

News Brief
The Pennsylvania Department of Health issued an alert on October 20, 2026, warning of a rise in measles infections among pregnant women and congenital measles cases. Since the outbreak began in April, 66 pregnant women in the state have been diagnosed with measles. The department noted that measles during pregnancy increases the risk of miscarriage, early labor, and low birth weight. Anecdotally, at least six babies were born with congenital measles, with two developing pneumonia and requiring intensive care. Congenital measles can lead to severe long-term complications, including subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), which is almost always fatal. The health department emphasized that there is no specific treatment for congenital measles, and prevention relies on vaccination before pregnancy. However, state law restricts disclosure of pregnancy outcomes, and the outbreak has been largely confined to Amish families, complicating data collection and transparency.
Original source text (English)
Oct. 20, 2026, 5:12 PM UTC By Erika Edwards The Pennsylvania Department of Health issued an alert Monday to doctors about an increase in measles infections in pregnant women, as well as a concerning rise in congenital measles — a disease that’s rarely, if ever, seen in the U.S. The department said that 66 pregnant women in Pennsylvania had been diagnosed with measles since the state’s outbreak began in April. “Getting measles while pregnant increases the risk of miscarriage, early labor, and low birth weight,” a department spokesperson said in an email. It’s unclear how many women have miscarried or delivered babies with congenital measles. The department said that state law prohibits it from releasing details of the outcomes of those pregnancies, and there is no national database that tracks cases. True numbers may also be obscured as the outbreak has largely been limited to Amish families who don’t always seek conventional medical assistance. As of Thursday, there have been 4,410 measles cases in the U.S. this year. In Pennsylvania , there have been 1,286 cases as of Monday. Even before widespread measles vaccination, congenital measles was rare. That’s because most women had already been infected as children. It’s only now emerging as a threat as measles outbreaks are increasing as vaccination rates fall. Last month, the American Academy of Pediatrics added a “frequently asked questions” page about congenital measles to its website in response to ongoing and increasing measles outbreaks. According to the AAP, congenital measles occurs in newborns whose mothers have been infected during the two weeks before birth and who test positive or develop measles symptoms within 10 days of delivery. Anecdotally, doctors have said that at least six babies were born in Pennsylvania with congenital measles. Babies can have a range of symptoms, from a rash and fever to pneumonia, neurological problems and low blood platelets. At least two developed pneumonia and were admitted to an intensive care unit, doctors said. Congenital measles can be deadly. One father in Lancaster County previously told NBC News that his wife was sick with measles in August and went into labor a month early. Their baby died shortly after birth. Congenital measles can also lead to severe brain and immune system issues later in life, the Pennsylvania Department of Health said. Those long-term complications can include a very rare and severe inflammatory brain condition called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, or SSPE . It can occur seven to 10 years after a measles infection and is almost always fatal. There is evidence that SSPE may occur earlier in children born with congenital measles. There’s no specific treatment for congenital measles other than supportive care, such as fluids, hydration and supplemental oxygen. The best way to prevent the newborn condition is for women to get vaccinated before getting pregnant, the health department said. Pregnant women who get measles are unable to get the vaccine because the shot contains a weakened but live version of the virus. Unvaccinated pregnant women who are exposed to measles can get a dose of immunoglobulin within six days of that exposure, the health department said, to “provide protection against measles infection or reduce the risk of serious illness among susceptible people.” The vaccine can be given to women after they deliver their babies and is safe for breastfeeding mothers, according to state health officials
View five news elements
Time
The Pennsylvania Department of Health issued an alert on Monday, October 20, 2026, regarding an increase in measles infections in pregnant women. As of Thursday, October 20, 2026, there have been 4,410 measles cases in the U.S., with 1,286 cases in Pennsylvania as of Monday, October 20, 2026.
Location and population
Pennsylvania, specifically targeting pregnant women and their newborns. The outbreak has largely been limited to Amish families who may not always seek conventional medical assistance.
Event
The Pennsylvania Department of Health reported an increase in measles infections among pregnant women and a concerning rise in congenital measles cases. The department warned that measles during pregnancy increases the risk of miscarriage, early labor, and low birth weight.
Scale, impact and risk
66 pregnant women in Pennsylvania had been diagnosed with measles since the outbreak began in April. Anecdotally, at least six babies were born with congenital measles, with at least two developing pneumonia and being admitted to an intensive care unit. The health department noted that congenital measles can be deadly and may lead to severe brain and immune system issues later in life, including subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), which is almost always fatal.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Pennsylvania Department of Health stated that state law prohibits releasing details of pregnancy outcomes, and there is no national database tracking cases. They emphasized the importance of vaccination before pregnancy and mentioned that immunoglobulin can be given to unvaccinated pregnant women within six days of exposure. The department also highlighted that the outbreak is largely limited to Amish families, which may affect data accuracy.

Measles outbreak in Bangladesh is a ‘real warning’ to other countries, experts say - NBC News

News Brief
Bangladesh is experiencing the world’s worst measles outbreak, with 202,000 suspected cases and 1,147 deaths, mostly among children under 5. The outbreak, which has more cases than any other country this year, highlights the risk of falling immunization rates. Despite swift emergency responses, challenges remain in reaching remote areas and addressing data gaps, offering a warning to other countries about the deadly consequences of low vaccination coverage.
Original source text (English)
Measles outbreak in Bangladesh is a ‘real warning’ to other countries, experts say IE 11 is not supported. For an optimal experience visit our site on another browser. Bangladesh, whose routine vaccination program faltered in 2025, has reported 202,000 suspected measles cases — 22,000 of them laboratory-confirmed — from the start of the outbreak in mid-March to Oct. 9, the World Health Organization said. That’s more suspected cases than any other country this year. So far, 1,147 people have died of the highly contagious disease, with 94% of reported deaths among children under 5 years old, the WHO said. Bangladesh’s experience shows the challenge of containing an outbreak when vaccination coverage falls below 95%, a level the WHO says is needed to prevent outbreaks. Bangladesh almost achieved that in recent years, but coverage dropped in 2025. 0 seconds of 13 secondsVolume 90% Press shift question mark to access a list of keyboard shortcuts Keyboard ShortcutsEnabledDisabled Play/PauseSPACE Increase Volume↑ Decrease Volume↓ Seek Forward→ Seek Backward← Captions On/Offc Fullscreen/Exit Fullscreenf Mute/Unmutem Decrease Caption Size- Increase Caption Size+ or = Seek %0-9 Play 0.5x1x1.25x1.5x2x Live 00:00 00:13 00:13 00:13 Pennsylvania’s measles outbreak shows no sign of slowing 00:0000:00 A Reuters review of WHO documents, alongside interviews with more than a dozen scientists, health workers and officials, shows that Bangladesh was also hampered in its emergency response by data that left out millions of children, shortcomings in preparation and a failure to reach enough parents. “This is a real warning to every country,” said Mark Jit, a WHO vaccine adviser and professor at the School of Global Public Health at New York University. Preventing an outbreak through vaccinations is easier, cheaper and safer than responding to an emergency, he said. “The response will stop the outbreak eventually, but in the meantime, many will have had measles, and some will have died,” he said. Ziauddin Hyder, special assistant on health affairs to Prime Minister Tarique Rahman, told Reuters in September that the government was doing everything it could to end the outbreak. The government did not respond to further questions sent in October for this article. ‘They came only after I buried my son’ In the northeast region of Sylhet, one of the worst-hit areas in the South Asian country of 178 million people, 2-year-old Arman Hossain died unvaccinated in July, 24 hours after admission to a hospital for treatment paid for by selling the family’s three-wheeled auto rickshaw. His unvaccinated 4-year-old sister survived. His father, Amin Mia, 26, said no health workers came to their remote village in Sylhet to offer vaccinations in time. “They came only after I buried my son,” he said. Measles usually kills two or three people in every 1,000 cases, the WHO says, but the disease can be more deadly in poorer countries because of factors such as malnutrition. In the town of Chhatak, also in the Sylhet region, the 9-month-old daughter of 30-year-old carpenter Humayun Rashid died unvaccinated in June. Labiba Akter could have received an inoculation in April when an emergency program lowered the vaccination age to 6 months from 9. “No health worker came to tell us,” her father said. In May, Health Minister Sardar Md. Sakhawat Husain said the emergency drive had brought cases in hot spots “almost to zero” and that the outbreak was under control. Yet in September, Bangladesh reported a daily average of 1,198 new cases of measles, which causes a high fever, cough and telltale rash. That was the highest total since May. The Bangladesh outbreak comes as vaccine hesitancy is rising, particularly in wealthier countries, amid skepticism championed by figures such as U.S. Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. In Bangladesh, access to inoculations and awareness are bigger hurdles, experts said. Until recently, Bangladesh had been a vaccination success story. The WHO and the United Nations children’s agency UNICEF estimated that in 2024 it achieved 96% coverage for the first vaccine dose and 93% for the second, which is given to capture the small percentage of children who don’t develop immunity after one shot. Measles usually kills two or three people in every 1,000 cases, the WHO says, but it can be deadlier in poorer countries because of factors such as malnutrition.Syed Mahamudur / NurPhoto via Getty But the same year, protests erupted that ended 15 years of rule by Prime Minister Sheikh Hasina. In the ensuing political turmoil, health workers went on strike, vaccine orders were delayed and follow-up vaccinations that usually accompany routine programs were disrupted. In a report published in July this year, WHO and UNICEF estimated that first-dose coverage in 2025 had fallen to 90%, while second-dose coverage was 86%, several percentage points below the 95% needed for herd immunity. The report also raised questions about the quality of official data on vaccinations for measles and other diseases for the years 2023 to 2025. It said preliminary figures sometimes showed numbers of those inoculated exceeded 100% of those targeted, indicating mistakes in counting doses or target numbers. Emergency program missed millions Still, when the measles outbreak erupted, Bangladesh responded swiftly and initially seemed to be winning the battle. An emergency program was followed by an initial slowdown in new cases after almost 20 million children received vaccinations in April and May. The government said it had covered every child in the target age group of 6 months to 5 years. But in reality, the program fell short. That emerged when another routine health intervention in June had a target list with 4 million more children than the measles campaign despite covering the same age group. “There is a gap,” prime ministerial adviser Hyder told Reuters on Sept. 14, as measles cases rose again, blaming vaccine hesitancy by parents or child illnesses for keeping children away. Other challenges have also emerged. Documents detailing meetings in April and August between the government, the WHO and other health partners, reviewed by Reuters and not previously reported, show how efforts to act fast may have led to shortcomings. Minutes from the April 19 meeting outlined how the government opted for just one round of so-called readiness checks before launching its emergency vaccination program due to time constraints, instead of the typical four rounds. Checks cover everything from ensuring fridges are available for keeping vaccines cool to determining how many children should be vaccinated. The single round of checks suggested that most — but not all — districts were ready, according to the minutes. The poorest ‘pay the price’ “When preparedness checks are not done properly, it is often the poorest and hardest-to-reach children who miss out on vaccination and pay the price,” said professor Mahmudur Rahman, an epidemiologist and chair of Bangladesh’s National Verification Committee for Measles and Rubella Elimination. Hyder said the April campaign was launched in four weeks. Yet Tajul Islam Abdul Bari, who led immunization in Bangladesh until 2017, said campaigns usually demand months of planning. By the August meeting, shortcomings in the rollout were identified. Minutes noted the proportion of children with confirmed measles cases who had not been vaccinated was “virtually unchanged between pre-campaign (82%) and post-campaign (85%) ... underscoring persistent coverage gaps.” The WHO did not respond to questions about the April and August meetings. But the deputy WHO representative to Bangladesh, Dr. Rajesh Narwal, said the agency was determined to support the government in its goal of vaccinating every child at risk. “Routine immunization must become more effective and resilient so that these gaps do not develop again,” he said. In Sylhet, where official records show 180 children have died, eight of 16 families Reuters interviewed in September said they had not seen health workers offering vaccinations. At least six families said health workers visited only after a child had died from measles in their community. At the end of September, the government launched a new campaign to target 3.4 million children previously missed. “We will involve people across villages so that no child is left behind,” the health minister told a news conference. Yet reaching every child remains a challenge. UNICEF and the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics say as many as 40% of children go unregistered, while government data showed that about a third of health worker posts are unfilled. Health experts are also pushing the government to expand the program to vaccinate children over 5. That age group represents a third of measles cases, the WHO says. A child patient at the Infectious Diseases Hospital in Dhaka in March.Abdul Goni / Drik via Getty In addition, scientists in Bangladesh are studying why immunity is waning faster than expected in babies up to 6 months or so. They are usually still protected by maternal antibodies, experts say. Ensuring prompt medical care is vital, the WHO says, another challenge for Bangladesh as it also fights a dengue epidemic. In September, every ward in Sylhet’s 100-bed Shahid Shamsuddin Ahmed Hospital, which is treating measles, appeared overwhelmed. On the day Reuters visited, 178 patients were admitted. 麻疹病毒 基因型 D8 在马来西亚占据主导地位,因为重复进口燃料本地 暴发 - Bioengineer.org

Measles Virus Genotype D8 Dominates Malaysia as Repeated Importations Fuel Local Outbreaks - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报马来西亚于 2026 年 10 月 18 日发表的一项新分子 麻疹病毒 研究显示,麻疹病毒 基因型 D8 在 2018 年至 2024 年间主导了该国的 暴发 格局。国家公共卫生实验室和卫生部的研究人员分析了 1,110 个病毒序列,发现基因型 D8 占所有检测到的 94%,基因型 B3 (4%) 和基因型 A (2%) 组成其余部分。研究表明,是从国外重复输入,然后是本地放大,而不是连续的国内传播。基因型 D8 于 2018 年在登嘉楼州首次检测到,并于 2023 年和 2024 年扩展到沙巴。系统发育和系统发育地理学分析表明沙巴和马来西亚半岛之间存在跨区域传播,强调了协调 监测 的必要性。大多数病例发生在未接种疫苗的儿童和青少年中,但 29% 的测序病例涉及之前接种过疫苗的个体,这引发了人们对免疫缺陷的担忧。作者警告说,如果不进行更密集的抽样,就不能明确排除低水平的国内 传播。该研究强调了解决 疫苗 犹豫、访问障碍和免疫力减弱的重要性,以防止进一步的 暴发。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究分析了麻疹病毒在2018年至2024年间的流通量,发布日期为2026年10月18日(N1、N18、N29)。
地点与对象
该研究重点关注马来西亚,特别是登嘉楼州(马来西亚半岛东海岸)和沙巴州(婆罗洲),重点关注未接种疫苗的儿童和青少年,以及以前接种过疫苗的个体(N18、N19、N24、N25)。
事件
一项分子 监测 研究发现,2018 年至 2024 年间,麻疹病毒 基因型 D8 在马来西亚占主导地位,反复输入可能会助长当地的 麻疹病毒。该研究使用系统发育和系统发育地理学分析来追踪病毒的传播(N1、N3、N4、N17、N37)。
规模、影响与风险
基因型 D8 占所有检测结果的 94%(1,110 个序列中的 1,043 个),而基因型 B3 占 4%,基因型 A 占 2%。该研究表明重复输入和局部扩增,其中 29% 的病例发生在先前接种过疫苗的个体中,这表明潜在的免疫缺口(N5、N6、N24、N25、N30)。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的结果基于系统发育和统计分析,但作者警告说,不能完全排除不间断的低水平国内 传播。建议在马来西亚的两个大陆上协调 监测 和响应(N4、N17、N38、N45)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles Virus Genotype D8 Dominates Malaysia as Repeated Importations Fuel Local Outbreaks - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A new molecular surveillance study from Malaysia, published on October 18, 2026, reveals that measles virus genotype D8 has dominated the country's outbreak landscape between 2018 and 2024. Researchers from the National Public Health Laboratory and the Ministry of Health analyzed 1,110 viral sequences, finding that genotype D8 accounted for 94% of all detections, with genotype B3 (4%) and genotype A (2%) making up the rest. The study suggests repeated importations from abroad, followed by local amplification, rather than continuous domestic transmission. Genotype D8 was first detected in Terengganu in 2018 and expanded to Sabah in 2023 and 2024. Phylogenetic and phylogeographic analyses indicate inter-regional spread between Sabah and Peninsular Malaysia, highlighting the need for coordinated surveillance. Most cases occurred among unvaccinated children and adolescents, but 29% of sequenced cases involved previously vaccinated individuals, raising concerns about immunity gaps. The authors caution that low-level domestic transmission cannot be definitively ruled out without more intensive sampling. The study underscores the importance of addressing vaccine hesitancy, access barriers, and waning immunity to prevent further outbreaks.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Sunday, October 18, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Measles Virus Genotype D8 Dominates Malaysia as Repeated Importations Fuel Local Outbreaks by October 18, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Measles Virus Genotype D8 Dominates Malaysia as Repeated Importations Fuel Local Outbreaks Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Measles remains one of the most contagious viral diseases known to medicine, and its persistence in countries with apparently high vaccine coverage continues to puzzle public health authorities. A new molecular surveillance study from Malaysia, published in BMC Infectious Diseases, has now provided the most detailed picture yet of how the measles virus circulated across the country between 2018 and 2024. Drawing on 1,110 viral sequences collected nationwide through routine surveillance and outbreak investigations, researchers from the National Public Health Laboratory and partner institutions within the Ministry of Health Malaysia traced the genetic fingerprint of the virus to determine whether measles was quietly endemic in the country or repeatedly arriving from abroad. Their answer, grounded in phylogenetic evidence, points strongly toward repeated importations followed by local amplification, although the authors caution that uninterrupted low-level domestic transmission cannot be entirely excluded. The study’s central finding is the overwhelming predominance of measles virus genotype D8, which accounted for 94 percent of all detections, representing 1,043 of the 1,110 sequences analysed. Genotype B3, another genotype commonly associated with outbreaks in Africa and South Asia, made up 4 percent of detections, while genotype A, which is vaccine-associated and typically signals detection of the attenuated strain contained in the measles vaccine, accounted for the remaining 2 percent. This genotype distribution is significant because the World Health Organization uses the genetic characteristics of the virus, particularly the nucleotide sequence encoding the carboxyl-terminal 450 nucleotides of the nucleoprotein gene, known as the N450 window, to classify measles virus strains and monitor their global movement. Genotyping within this framework allows epidemiologists to distinguish between viral lineages circulating endemically in a region and those imported from countries where measles remains endemic. Discover more Learn Tagalog Read Travel Guides Vaccine To assess temporal trends, the researchers applied logistic regression to the genotype data and found that the odds of detecting genotype D8 increased nearly fourfold per year across the study period, a statistically robust association with a p-value below 0.0001. This steep rise in D8 representation suggests that the lineage became progressively entrenched in the national viral landscape over the seven-year window. Chi-squared tests were used to compare genotype distributions across time and geography, providing a quantitative backbone for the phylogenetic analyses that followed. The combination of classical statistical inference with modern phylogenetic tooling reflects a methodological approach increasingly standard in viral genomic epidemiology, where the goal is not merely to count cases but to reconstruct the transmission history of a pathogen from its genetic sequence data. Discover more Check Symptoms Study Biology Order DNA Kits Buy Maps Phylogenetic analyses were performed using the Nextstrain Augur pipeline, an open-source bioinformatics framework widely adopted for real-time pathogen genomic surveillance. The researchers contextualised their Malaysian sequences alongside 28 World Health Organization reference strains, enabling them to place local viral diversity within the global phylogenetic tree of measles virus. The analyses revealed that Malaysian genotype D8 sequences formed a highly conserved lineage, with nucleotide identity ranging from 99.2 to 99.9 percent across the sampled viruses. Molecular clock analyses, which estimate the timing of viral divergence based on the accumulation of mutations over time, further supported the interpretation that multiple phylogenetically distinct clusters appeared across the study period. The presence of these separate clusters, rather than a single continuously evolving lineage, is the key molecular signature that the authors cite as evidence compatible with repeated introductions of the virus from external sources, each followed by local amplification within susceptible populations. Within the sequenced dataset, genotype D8 was first detected in the state of Terengganu in 2018, on the east coast of Peninsular Malaysia. Over the subsequent years, the lineage expanded its geographic footprint, with increased representation in Sabah during 2023 and 2024. Phylogeographic patterns, which combine genetic relatedness with geographic metadata to reconstruct viral movement, were compatible with inter-regional spread involving both Sabah and Peninsular Malaysia. This finding is epidemiologically important because Sabah, one of Malaysia’s two states on the island of Borneo, has historically faced challenges in vaccination delivery due to geographic remoteness, undocumented migration, and variable healthcare access. The genetic evidence of viral movement between Sabah and the peninsula underscores the interconnectedness of Malaysia’s measles epidemiology across its two landmasses separated by the South China Sea. Discover more Find Vaccines vaccine public health The demographic profile of measles cases in the study reveals a critical vulnerability in Malaysia’s immunisation landscape. Most cases occurred among unvaccinated children and adolescents, indicating that the primary driver of measles transmission is the presence of individuals who have not received the recommended doses of measles-containing vaccine. However, a notable 29 percent of sequenced cases occurred in previously vaccinated individuals, a proportion that warrants careful interpretation. Vaccine failure can arise from primary vaccine failure, where an individual fails to develop an adequate immune response after vaccination, or secondary vaccine failure, where immunity wanes over time. Alternatively, some vaccinated cases may reflect incorrect vaccination records or incomplete dose history. The relatively high proportion of breakthrough cases, if confirmed through serological assessment, could suggest gaps in population-level immunity that extend beyond the unvaccinated population. Malaysia has reported high measles vaccine coverage through its National Immunisation Programme, which provides two doses of measles-containing vaccine to children at nine months and twelve months of age. The persistence of measles despite this coverage highlights the concept of immunity gaps, where pockets of susceptible individuals, whether due to vaccine hesitancy, access barriers, migration, or waning immunity, create conditions sufficient for viral amplification when the virus is introduced. The WHO framework for measles elimination requires that a country demonstrate the absence of endemic measles virus transmission for at least 36 consecutive months, with all cases confirmed as importation-related through molecular surveillance. The Malaysian study’s finding that molecular patterns are compatible with repeated introductions rather than endemic persistence is therefore encouraging from an elimination standpoint, as it suggests that the country may be closer to interrupting transmission than case numbers alone would indicate. The distinction between endemic persistence and repeated importation is not merely academic. It has direct implications for public health strategy. If measles were endemic, the priority would be to strengthen routine immunisation and conduct supplementary immunisation activities to close immunity gaps. If measles is primarily imported, the emphasis shifts toward border health measures, rapid outbreak response, contact tracing, and targeted catch-up vaccination in communities with identified susceptibility. The Malaysian study’s phylogenetic evidence, showing multiple distinct clusters appearing and disappearing over time rather than a single continuously evolving lineage, supports the latter interpretation. However, the authors appropriately acknowledge the limitation that low-level domestic transmission, occurring below the detection threshold of the surveillance system, cannot be definitively ruled out without more intensive sampling and sequencing. The study also contributes to the broader regional picture of measles epidemiology in Southeast Asia, where several countries have experienced measles resurgence in recent years following disruptions to immunisation services during the COVID-19 pandemic. The WHO South-East Asia and Western Pacific regions have set elimination goals, and molecular surveillance is a cornerstone of the verification process. The Malaysian dataset, spanning seven years and over a thousand sequences, represents one of the larger national measles sequence collections in the region and provides a valuable reference for neighbouring countries conducting their own phylogenetic analyses. The researchers acknowledge the WHO Measles Nucleotide Surveillance database, known as MeaNS, for providing the reference sequences used to contextualise their findings within the global measles virus phylogeny. Looking forward, the study’s findings suggest several priorities for strengthening measles control in Malaysia. Closing immunity gaps among unvaccinated children and adolescents remains the most direct intervention, and the identification of specific states and age groups where susceptibility is concentrated provides actionable targets for immunisation campaigns. The phylogeographic evidence of inter-regional spread between Sabah and Peninsular Malaysia highlights the need for coordinated surveillance and response across both landmasses. Continued investment in molecular surveillance, including routine sequencing of measles virus detections and integration of phylogenetic analyses into national outbreak investigations, will be essential for verifying elimination status and responding rapidly to future importations. The research received no specific external funding and was conducted using anonymised surveillance data from the National Public Health Laboratory, with ethical approval from the Medical Research and Ethics Committee of the Ministry of Health Malaysia. The study focused on Malaysia, specifically the states of Terengganu (east coast of Peninsular Malaysia) and Sabah (Borneo), with an emphasis on unvaccinated children and adolescents, as well as previously vaccinated individuals (N18, N19, N24, N25).
Event
A molecular surveillance study found that measles virus genotype D8 dominated Malaysia between 2018 and 2024, with repeated importations likely fueling local outbreaks. The study used phylogenetic and phylogeographic analyses to trace the virus's spread (N1, N3, N4, N17, N37).
Scale, impact and risk
Genotype D8 accounted for 94% of all detections (1,043 out of 1,110 sequences), while genotype B3 made up 4% and genotype A 2%. The study suggests repeated importations and local amplification, with a notable 29% of cases in previously vaccinated individuals, indicating potential immunity gaps (N5, N6, N24, N25, N30).

The study's findings are based on phylogenetic and statistical analyses, but the authors caution that uninterrupted low-level domestic transmission cannot be entirely excluded. Coordinated surveillance and response across Malaysia's two landmasses are recommended (N4, N17, N38, N45).

宾夕法尼亚州对先天性 麻疹 发出临床警报 — Ars Technica - UA.NEWS

Pennsylvania issues clinical alert over congenital measles — Ars Technica - UA.NEWS
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚州卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 20 日发布了关于怀孕期间 麻疹 病例和新生儿相关先天性 麻疹 病例增加的临床警报。这标志着一个重大的公共卫生问题,因为美国已经几十年没有记录此类病例了。截至 2026 年 10 月 19 日,42 个县报告了 1,286 例 麻疹 病例,其中 247 例住院治疗,6 例死亡,其中 1 例死亡仍在审查中。怀孕期间的 麻疹 会增加严重并发症的风险,包括住院、肺炎和死亡,以及流产、死产和低出生体重。该州建议接触该病毒的孕妇在六天内接受静脉注射免疫球蛋白。怀孕期间不建议使用 麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹 疫苗。该病毒可在子宫内或分娩过程中传播给胎儿,因此需要提高警惕并采取预防措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 20 日发布了临床警报。截至 2026 年 10 月 19 日,暴发 自年初以来一直在持续进行。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国宾夕法尼亚州,影响了42个县。受影响人群包括孕妇和新生儿,重点是先天性麻疹病例。
事件
由于怀孕期间 麻疹 病例以及新生儿先天性 麻疹 相关病例的增加,宾夕法尼亚州发布了临床警报。这是根据州卫生部门的报告确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月19日,42个县共有1,286例麻疹病例。其中 247 人住院治疗,六人死亡,但其中 1 例仍在审查中。怀孕期间的 麻疹 会增加住院、肺炎、氧气支持、机械通气和死亡的风险。它还与流产、死产、低出生体重和早产有关。
应对、状态与不确定性
州卫生部门正在审查最新的死亡病例,然后将其添加到官方统计数据中。建议接触该病毒的孕妇在六天内接受静脉注射免疫球蛋白。怀孕期间不建议使用 麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹 疫苗。该病毒可以在子宫内或分娩过程中传播给胎儿。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pennsylvania issues clinical alert over congenital measles — Ars Technica - UA.NEWS

News Brief
The Pennsylvania Department of Health issued a clinical alert on 20 October 2026 regarding a rise in measles cases during pregnancy and related congenital measles cases in newborns. This marks a significant public health concern as such cases had not been recorded in the United States for decades. As of October 19, 2026, 1,286 measles cases were reported across 42 counties, with 247 hospitalizations and six deaths, though one death is still under review. Measles during pregnancy increases the risk of severe complications, including hospitalization, pneumonia, and death, as well as miscarriage, stillbirth, and low birth weight. The state advises pregnant women exposed to the virus to receive intravenous immunoglobulin within six days. The measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine is not recommended during pregnancy. The virus can be transmitted to the fetus in utero or during delivery, highlighting the need for heightened vigilance and preventive measures.
Original source text (English)
News Health Pennsylvania issues clinical alert over congenital measles — Ars Technica Fedir Kryshtovskyi 20 October 2026 02:52 The Pennsylvania Department of Health in the United States has issued a clinical alert for medical professionals due to a rise in measles cases during pregnancy and related cases of congenital measles in newborns. Before the current outbreak, such cases had not been recorded in the United States for decades, Ars Technica reports . Scale of the outbreak As of October 19, Pennsylvania had recorded 1,286 measles cases in 42 counties since the beginning of the year. Of these, 247 people were hospitalized. Six deaths were reported during the outbreak, although the latest of these cases is still being reviewed by the state health department before being added to official statistics. In early October, The 19th reported 53 cases of measles during pregnancy. At least one case of congenital measles resulted in death: a newborn boy died immediately after birth while his mother was severely ill with measles. More current news is available on the UA.News Telegram channel Telegram . Risks for pregnant women and infants Measles during pregnancy increases the risk of hospitalization, pneumonia, the need for oxygen support or mechanical ventilation, and death. The infection is also associated with a higher risk of miscarriage, stillbirth, low birth weight, and premature birth. The measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine is not recommended during pregnancy because it is a live attenuated vaccine. Pregnant women without measles vaccination or a previous infection who have been exposed to the virus are advised to receive intravenous immunoglobulin within six days of exposure. The virus can be transmitted to the fetus in utero or infect a child during or immediately after delivery. In infants, congenital measles may manifest as fever, jaundice, rash, low platelet levels, pneumonia, and neurological complications. Children who survive the acute infection require long-term monitoring due to the risk of immune amnesia and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, a progressive fatal neurological disease that can occur years after infection. It is also linked to miscarriage, stillbirth, low birth weight, and premature birth.

The virus can be transmitted to the fetus in utero or during delivery.

随着 麻疹 覆盖率下降,土耳其 麻疹 病例激增超过 3,000 例 - Hürriyet Daily News

Measles cases surge past 3,000 in Türkiye as vaccination coverage falls - Hürriyet Daily News
新闻简报In October 2026, measles cases in Türkiye surged past 3,000, according to the Turkish Medical Association and infectious disease specialists. This marks a significant increase from 127 cases in 2022, 500 in 2023, 1,500 in 2024, and around 2,000 in 2025. The outbreak is attributed to declining vaccination coverage, vaccine hesitancy, and gaps in immunization tracking. Experts note that two doses of the measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine provide approximately 97-99 percent protection, but coverage has reportedly fallen to around 92 percent, below the 95 percent threshold needed to prevent sustained transmission. The measles virus is highly contagious, with a single patient potentially infecting 16 to 18 people. Around 30 percent of reported cases develop serious complications such as pneumonia, encephalitis, and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE). The situation remains under investigation, with concerns over the risk of further outbreaks and the impact on public health.
查看新闻五要素
时间
视频播放器正在加载。
地点与对象
土耳其,重点关注普通人群,包括成人和儿童。
事件
据土耳其医学协会和传染病专家报告,2026 年 10 月,土耳其的 麻疹 病例已激增至近 3,000 例。这些消息来源引用的数据证实了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has seen cases rise from 127 in 2022 to 500 in 2023, 1,500 in 2024, and around 2,000 in 2025. Around 30 percent of reported cases develop complications such as pneumonia, encephalitis, and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE). The measles virus is highly contagious, with a single patient potentially infecting 16 to 18 people.
应对、状态与不确定性
专家将这种复苏归因于 疫苗接种 覆盖率下降、疫苗 犹豫不决以及免疫追踪方面的差距。麻疹-腮腺炎-风疹 (MMR) 疫苗 通过土耳其国家免疫计划免费接种,计划在 12 个月大时接种,并在 4 至 6 岁时再次接种。目前情况正在调查中,人们担心进一步出现 暴发 的风险。

Advertisement Next Stay Close ✕ Ad loading Haberin Devamı The highly contagious virus can infect 16 to 18 people through a single patient, making it one of the most transmissible infectious diseases. Cases in Türkiye rose from 127 in 2022 to 500 in 2023, 1,500 in 2024 and around 2,000 in 2025, according to World Health Organization data. Experts say falling vaccination coverage, vaccine hesitancy and gaps in immunization tracking are contributing to the resurgence. Measles vaccination coverage in Türkiye has reportedly fallen to around 92 percent, below the 95 percent threshold considered necessary to prevent sustained transmission. The measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine is administered free of charge through Türkiye’s national immunization program, with doses scheduled at 12 months and again at age four to six. Two doses provide protection to approximately 97-99 percent of recipients, experts say. Infectious disease specialist Songül Özer warned that the disease is increasingly affecting adults as well as children and can cause serious complications. Around 30 percent of reported cases develop at least one complication such as pneumonia, encephalitis (inflammation of the brain) and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), according to experts.
