Video Player is loading.
Location and population
Türkiye, with a focus on the general population, including adults and children.
Event
Measles cases in Türkiye have surged to nearly 3,000 in October 2026, as reported by the Turkish Medical Association and infectious disease specialists. This is confirmed based on figures cited by these sources.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has seen cases rise from 127 in 2022 to 500 in 2023, 1,500 in 2024, and around 2,000 in 2025. Around 30 percent of reported cases develop complications such as pneumonia, encephalitis, and subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE). The measles virus is highly contagious, with a single patient potentially infecting 16 to 18 people.
Response, status and uncertainty
Experts attribute the resurgence to falling vaccination coverage, vaccine hesitancy, and gaps in immunization tracking. The measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) vaccine is administered free of charge through Türkiye’s national immunization program, with doses scheduled at 12 months and again at age four to six. The situation is under investigation, with concerns over the risk of further outbreaks.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

US surpasses 1,000 measles cases for the 3rd time in 26 years, CDC data shows - ABC7 Bay Area

US surpasses 1,000 measles cases for the 3rd time in 26 years, CDC data shows - ABC7 Bay Area
新闻简报根据 CDC 截至 2026 年 2 月 27 日的数据,美国 麻疹 病例 26 年来第三次超过 1,000 例。这一激增包括上周至少新增 154 例,使总数达到 1,136 例。暴发 主要是由南卡罗来纳州的一个大型 暴发 推动的,截至该日期,该地区已记录 979 例病例。CDC 报告称,92% 的病例属于未接种疫苗或 疫苗接种 状态未知的个体,其中 4% 的人接受了一剂 MMR 疫苗,4% 的人接受了两剂。暴发 引起了人们对 疫苗接种 比率的担忧,因为在 2024-2025 学年期间,只有 92.5% 的幼儿园儿童获得了 MMR 疫苗,低于往年。CDC 建议接种两剂 MMR 疫苗,第一剂在 12 至 15 个月大时注射,第二剂在 4 至 6 岁时注射。目前正在积极调查这一情况并采取公共卫生应对措施,这突出表明需要改善 麻疹 的覆盖范围,以减轻 麻疹 的传播和影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发布于2026年10月23日。美国麻疹病例26年来第三次突破1,000例,上周至少确诊154例新病例,截至2026年2月27日总计1,136例。
地点与对象
美国 27 个州报告了 麻疹 暴发 疫情,其中包括南卡罗来纳州,该州去年开始大规模 麻疹 疫情,截至 2026 年 2 月 27 日已发生 979 例病例。受影响人群包括未接种疫苗的个人和 疫苗接种 状态未知的人,约占病例的 92%。
事件
CDC数据证实,美国的麻疹病例26年来第三次超过1000例。这归因于南卡罗来纳州去年开始的大规模 暴发 活动,并已蔓延到其他州。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年2月27日,美国已有1,136例麻疹确诊病例,其中南卡罗来纳州有979例。CDC 报告称,92% 的病例属于未接种疫苗或 疫苗接种 状态未知的个体,4% 的病例接种了一剂 MMR 疫苗,而 4% 的病例接种了两剂。暴发 引起了人们对 疫苗接种 费率和公共卫生风险的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在监测 暴发 并推荐两剂 MMR 疫苗。联邦数据显示,近年来 疫苗接种 率一直滞后,2024-2025 学年,92.5% 的幼儿园学生获得 MMR 疫苗,低于往年。目前正在积极调查情况并采取公共卫生应对措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

US surpasses 1,000 measles cases for the 3rd time in 26 years, CDC data shows - ABC7 Bay Area

News Brief
The U.S. has surpassed 1,000 measles cases for the third time in 26 years, according to CDC data as of February 27, 2026. This surge includes at least 154 new cases in the last week, bringing the total to 1,136. The outbreak is primarily driven by a large outbreak in South Carolina, which recorded 979 cases as of that date. The CDC reports that 92% of cases are among unvaccinated or unknown vaccination status individuals, with 4% having received one dose of the MMR vaccine and 4% having received two doses. The outbreak has raised concerns about vaccination rates, as only 92.5% of kindergartners received the MMR vaccine during the 2024-2025 school year, lower than previous years. The CDC recommends two doses of the MMR vaccine, with the first at 12 to 15 months and the second between 4 and 6 years old. The situation is under active investigation and public health response, highlighting the need for improved vaccination coverage to mitigate the spread and impact of measles.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LiveSan FranciscoEast BaySouth BayPeninsulaNorth Bay 67° Measles US surpasses 1,000 measles cases for the 3rd time in 26 years, CDC data shows Cases have been confirmed in 27 states, CDC data shows. Watch ABC News live news stream and get 24/7 latest, breaking news coverage, and live video. ByMary Kekatos Friday, February 27, 2026 Share Measles cases have topped 1,000 in the United States for the third time in 26 years. At least 154 new measles cases have been confirmed in the last week for a total of 1,136, according to updated data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). So far this year, cases have been confirmed in 27 states: Arizona, California, Colorado, Florida, Georgia, Idaho, Illinois, Kentucky, Maine, Minnesota, Nebraska, New Mexico, New York, North Carolina, North Dakota, Ohio, Oklahoma, Oregon, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, South Dakota, Texas, Utah, Vermont, Virginia, Washington and Wisconsin. Just six measles cases were reported among international travelers so far this year, according to CDC data. About 92% of cases are among people who are unvaccinated or whose vaccination status is unknown, CDC data shows. Meanwhile, 4% of cases are among those who have received just one dose of the measles, mumps, rubella (MMR) vaccine and 4% of cases are among those who received the recommended two doses, according to the CDC. The current measles situation in the U.S. is partly being driven by a large outbreak in South Carolina that began last year, with 979 cases recorded as of Friday, according to state health officials. Top Stories El Niño: SF declares local emergency to prepare for potential severe storms, coastal flooding $300K reward offered in 1987 Daly City cold case murder of Michelle Matteucci 1 minute ago 2 killed in 5-car crash on I-280 in Los Altos after driver crossed into oncoming traffic: CHP Last year, the U.S. recorded 2,281 measles cases, which is the highest number of national cases in 33 years, according to the CDC. Before that, the last time measles cases rose above 1,000 was 2019 -- CDC data showed 1,274 that year. Recorded cases dropped to a low of 13 in 2020, the first year of the COVID-19 pandemic. It also marked the first U.S. deaths recorded from measles in a decade, two among school-aged unvaccinated children in Texas and a third of an unvaccinated adult in New Mexico. The CDC currently recommends people receive two doses of the MMR vaccine, the first at ages 12 to 15 months and the second between 4 and 6 years old. One dose is 93% effective, and two doses are 97% effective against measles, according to the CDC. However, federal data shows vaccination rates have been lagging in recent years. During the 2024-2025 school year, 92.5% of kindergartners received the MMR vaccine, according to data. This is lower than the 92.7% seen in the previous school year and the 95.2% seen in the 2019-2020 school year, before the COVID-19 pandemic. DuPage County measles case prompts exposure alert at Chicago O'Hare International Airport - ABC7 Chicago
新闻简报伊利诺伊州杜佩奇县确诊的 麻疹 病例已促使芝加哥奥黑尔国际机场发布暴露警报。该人已接种疫苗,在国际旅行后检测出 麻疹 呈阳性,这导致人们担心 10 月 15 日下午 4:20 至 5 号航站楼可能存在暴露风险。下午 5:20卫生官员强调,公众面临的风险仍然很低,但他们敦促任何可能接触过病毒的人监测发烧、皮疹、咳嗽、流鼻涕和眼睛发红、流泪等症状,这些症状可能在接触后 21 天内出现。他们还建议在访问设施之前联系医疗保健提供者,以帮助防止进一步传播。该病例突显了美国 麻疹 病例的持续增加,2026 年报告了 4410 例,而 2025 年报告了 2289 例。官员们强调了 麻疹 在降低突破性病例可能性方面的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

DuPage County measles case prompts exposure alert at Chicago O'Hare International Airport - ABC7 Chicago

News Brief
A confirmed measles case in DuPage County, Illinois, has prompted an exposure alert at Chicago O'Hare International Airport. The individual, who is vaccinated, tested positive for measles after international travel, leading to concerns about potential exposure in Terminal 5 on October 15 between 4:20 p.m. and 5:20 p.m. Health officials emphasize that the risk to the public remains low, but they are urging anyone who may have been exposed to monitor for symptoms such as fever, rash, cough, runny nose, and red, watery eyes, which can develop up to 21 days after exposure. They also advise contacting healthcare providers before visiting facilities to help prevent further spread. This case highlights the ongoing rise in measles cases in the U.S., with 4410 reported in 2026 compared to 2289 in 2025. Officials stress the importance of vaccination in reducing the likelihood of breakthrough cases.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LiveChicago & Suburban Cook Co.North SuburbsWest SuburbsSouth SuburbsNW Indiana 63° Measles DuPage County measles case prompts exposure alert at Chicago's O'Hare airport A DuPage County measles case has prompted an exposure alert at Chicago O'Hare International Airport, health officials said. ByCate Cauguiran Friday, October 23, 2026 3:31AM Share CHICAGO (WLS) -- Chicago and DuPage County health officials are urging people who may have been exposed to measles to watch for symptoms after what officials describe as a rare breakthrough case involving a vaccinated DuPage County resident. ABC7 Chicago is now streaming 24/7. Click here to watch The first confirmed measles case in DuPage County this year has prompted an exposure alert at O'Hare International Airport. Public health officials say anyone who was in Terminal 5 on Oct. 15 between 4:20 p.m. and 7 p.m. may have been exposed after a DuPage County resident tested positive for measles following international travel. Health officials are encouraging anyone who may have been at the airport during that time to monitor for symptoms and know what steps to take if they become ill. "If you suspect that you have contracted measles or that you've been exposed to measles, call ahead if you're going to go to an emergency room or urgent care. Call ahead so they can establish some isolation protocols," said Dr. Constantine Kanakis, a clinical pathologist at Northwestern Memorial Hospital. According to health officials, symptoms of measles can include fever, rash, cough, runny nose and red, watery eyes. Officials say symptoms may develop up to 21 days after exposure. "Vaccines offer excellent, but not perfect protection from things like measles and other viruses. And the more people that are vaccinated, the less breakthrough cases happen," Kanakis said. The DuPage County case comes as measles cases continue to rise across the United States. "The data shows that cases are indeed going up. Last year, at the same time, there were 2289 cases last fall. The same calendar time this year in 2026, there are almost double that, 4410 cases per the CDC," Kanakis said. Health officials say anyone who develops symptoms after a potential exposure should contact a healthcare provider before going to a doctor's office, urgent care facility or emergency room to help prevent additional exposures and allow healthcare facilities to take appropriate precautions. 麻疹 正在切斯特县蔓延,但许多学校没有谈论其风险 - Inquirer.com

Measles is spreading in Chester County, but many schools aren’t talking about their risk - Inquirer.com
新闻简报该文章报道了 麻疹 暴发 在宾夕法尼亚州切斯特县蔓延的情况,西切斯特地区学区的几所学校面临着 疫苗接种 率下降的问题。该学区 11 所小学中有 5 所的幼儿园 MMR 疫苗接种 率低于 95% 的群体免疫阈值,其中 Penn Wood 小学为 87.5%。三所私立学校也报告了较低的入学率。尽管存在这些担忧,学校领导尚未向社区传达这一风险,学区护士也仅联系了没有 疫苗接种 记录的家庭。健康专家警告说,即使 疫苗接种 比率小幅下降也会增加社区风险,尤其是在流行病期间。暴发 造成了干扰,例如学校停课一天以核实员工 疫苗接种 记录。公共卫生官员指出,麻疹 在该地区正在积极传播,预计学校将在传播有关 麻疹 的信息方面发挥作用。然而,一些学校领导不愿直接解决这个问题,担心可能因各种原因选择退出 疫苗接种 的家长的强烈反对。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年10月19日。麻疹 暴发 正在进行中,报告的 疫苗接种 比率截至上学年(2025 年)。事件日期没有明确说明,但 暴发 被描述为蔓延到全州并进入切斯特县。
地点与对象
该活动以切斯特县为中心,特别是西切斯特地区学区,约有 12,000 名学生。受影响的人群包括该地区的幼儿园学生和其他学生,一些学校报告 疫苗接种 率低于群体免疫 95% 的阈值。
事件
麻疹 麻疹 正在切斯特县蔓延,西切斯特地区学区的几所学校报告幼儿园 麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹 (MMR) 疫苗接种 比率低于 95% 的阈值。暴发 是更大的全国趋势的一部分,健康专家警告说,即使 疫苗接种 比率小幅下降也会增加社区风险。
规模、影响与风险
该学区 11 所小学中有 5 所报告幼儿园 MMR 疫苗接种 率低于 95%,其中 Penn Wood 小学为 87.5%。三所私立学校的学费也相当低。健康专家指出,即使是 96% 的 疫苗接种 率也不能保证在流行病期间提供保护。暴发 导致了中断,例如学校停课一天以验证 疫苗接种 记录。
应对、状态与不确定性
西切斯特的学校领导尚未发布有关 麻疹 暴发 风险的社区信息。学区护士已联系没有 疫苗接种 记录的家庭,另外一些学生也已接种疫苗。然而,学校不愿意公开讨论 麻疹,公共卫生官员也对该地区的 麻疹 流通发出警告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles is spreading in Chester County, but many schools aren’t talking about their risk - Inquirer.com

News Brief
The article reports on a measles outbreak spreading in Chester County, Pennsylvania, with several schools in the West Chester Area School District facing declining vaccination rates. Kindergarten MMR vaccination rates in five of the district’s 11 elementary schools fell below the 95% threshold for herd immunity, with Penn Wood Elementary at 87.5%. Three private schools also reported low rates. Despite these concerns, school leaders have not communicated the risk to the community, and district nurses have only contacted families without vaccination records. Health experts warn that even small declines in vaccination rates can increase community risk, especially during an epidemic. The outbreak has caused disruptions, such as a school shutdown for a day to verify staff vaccination records. Public health officials note that measles is actively circulating in the area, and schools are expected to play a role in disseminating information about the outbreak. However, some school leaders are hesitant to address the issue directly, fearing backlash from parents who may have opted out of vaccinations for various reasons.
Original source text (English)
As the nation's worst measles outbreak spreads, the West Chester Area School District is among those facing declining vaccination rates — and challenges in preparing. Lynn April has seen chatter on social media from parents in her community, expressing hesitancy about giving their children vaccines. But she was shocked to hear that numerous elementary schools in the West Chester Area School District had seen crucial immunization rates fall, as a vaccine-preventable measles outbreak expands into Chester County . “I would think the majority of parents I am associated with would also find that pretty horrifying,” said April, who has a third grader and a fifth grader at East Goshen Elementary. The suburban district reflects a trend worrying health experts: Fewer families are choosing to vaccinate their kids. In West Chester, five of the district’s 11 elementary schools reported kindergarten measles, mumps, and rubella vaccination rates last school year below 95% — the threshold long considered necessary to ensure community protection. One school, Penn Wood Elementary, appeared especially high risk, with only 87.5% vaccinated. Three private schools in or on the border of the district also reported concerningly low kindergarten MMR vaccination rates. Yet school leaders in West Chester have not issued community messages highlighting the risk of the current measles outbreak. Nor are they sharing school-by-school vaccination data that indicate risky pockets within a district of about 12,000 students that touts an overall high vaccination rate. West Chester Superintendent David Christopher questioned whether his community remains as exposed as state data collected last school year suggest. District nurses have called families that didn’t have vaccination records on file, Christopher said, and accounting for all grade levels, every school campus has a vaccination rate above 95%. “We’re really talking about a pretty small percentage of students in the district,” Christopher said in an interview. Even small declines in vaccination rates within a grade level, however, can increase community risk, health experts said. With the nation’s largest measles outbreak in decades spreading across the state, René Najera, director of public health at the College of Physicians of Philadelphia, says a high vaccination rate no longer creates a comfort zone. “It’s some comfort, but it’s not enough, especially in an epidemic situation,” Najera said. “You could have a population with 96% vaccinated, and still have a roaring infection go through.” Christopher said he’s aware the ongoing measles outbreak could bring significant disruption: Earlier this month, a reported measles case led Oxford Area High School to shut down for a day to verify vaccination records for staff members. “The nature of it — you could have one student in a building, and the entire building exposed — is concerning,” Christopher said. Across the Philadelphia region, vaccination rates have been dropping, with at least 40% of area schools reporting kindergarten measles vaccination rates in 2025 below the threshold for what scientists call “herd immunity.” Kindergarten vaccination rates are considered a bellwether for a community’s attitudes toward vaccination, as parents are deciding whether to get the shots required by law to attend school. And increasingly, families are refusing vaccines . Pennsylvania has some of the loosest vaccine exemption policies in the nation, broadly allowing families to pass on immunizations required for school entry by claiming religious, philosophical, or medical objections. At West Chester’s Penn Wood Elementary, MMR vaccination rates among kindergartners fell 8 percentage points last year, with 56 out of 64 kindergartners vaccinated. Five students had philosophical exemptions to the vaccine, according to state data. (Another four were “noncompliant but attending,” a term the state uses for children who haven’t submitted exemption records but are in school.) At Westtown-Thornbury, MMR vaccinations dropped from 100% of kindergartners two years ago to under 92% last year — seemingly because of seven students claiming religious exemptions to vaccination. Sue Gramling, the president of Westtown-Thornbury’s parent-teacher organization, declined to comment about the school’s rate, saying in a message that she didn’t want to speak on behalf of the PTO “since this is such a hot topic right now.” Two other West Chester elementaries with kindergarten MMR rates below the 95% threshold, Glen Acres and East Goshen, also recorded declines in the past year. Christopher said those rates are dated. Nurses later called families who hadn’t submitted vaccination paperwork, more provided records, and some additional students have been vaccinated, he said. Asked whether nurses had called families who opted out through legal exemptions, Christopher said in an email that “the connections have been to ensure that families are educated to make informed decisions about vaccines.” “We understand that there are a variety of reasons that families may make this decision, and it is not our intention to pressure families, but to make sure they are informed,” he said. As community organizations, schools have a responsibility to help disseminate information that affects their students and staff, said Holley Wilkin, an associate professor of communications at Georgia State University who specializes in health communication. “With something like measles, it’s affecting their students and they should be playing that role,” she said. To help communities understand their risk, Pennsylvania’s health department published school-level vaccination data this summer for the first time. » READ MORE: More Philly-area classrooms are seeing lower measles vaccination rates. How protected is your school? Public health officials have warned since the summer that measles is actively circulating along Chester County’s western border with Lancaster County, the center of the state’s current outbreak. But schools appear reluctant to talk about measles planning. Of 12 Chester County school districts contacted by The Inquirer about their preparations, only West Chester agreed to an interview. In the Downingtown Area School District — where two schools last year reported kindergarten MMR vaccination rates last year below herd immunity levels — spokesperson Jenn Shealy said the district had communicated with staff about possible quarantines. “Our nurses have also individually reached out to the small number of families of students for whom we do not have current vaccination records,” Shealy said in an email. The Kennett Consolidated School District has “communicated with families and staff that immunizations for measles are a protective measure for all,” spokesperson Lisa Palmarini said in an email. Some area school leaders who faced backlash over pandemic restrictions acknowledged tension around communicating about measles. Marc Bertrando, superintendent of the Garnet Valley School District in Delaware County , said he wanted to make sure the district communicated with medically fragile students about the outbreak. But he didn’t want to be seen as dictating medical decisions. “We’re just going to stick to, ‘Everybody makes their own health choices, but these are the facts,’” Bertrando said in an interview last month. Wilkin, the communications expert, said schools that ignore their role as public health messengers also run the risk of angering parents of children who can’t be vaccinated — and rely on so-called herd immunity to keep them safe. “There’s going to be potentially some parents upset at you no matter which way you go,” she said. In the event of a measles case reported in a school building, Chester County health officials say all exposed students and staff who can’t prove vaccination will have to stay home for 21 days. West Chester has created a secure portal for staff to upload vaccination records, Christopher said. “The hard part is just finding those records,” Christopher said. He said the “vast majority” of staff had been vaccinated, but couldn’t provide an estimate of how many staff had provided documentation. The president of the district’s teachers union, Michele Curay-Cramer, declined to comment on measles preparations. April, the East Goshen elementary parent, was surprised to hear about possible 21-day quarantines. “That’s a long time to have a teacher out,” she said. In the Oxford Area School District, the decision to shut down the high school Oct. 9 also surprised community members. Some said they wanted more information about why the district had closed the school but not called off a high school football game. “I’m a bit taken aback,” said Joe Maida, whose 5-year-old granddaughter attends a district elementary school. But “I’m not giving it much energy.” Christopher said he isn’t sure how many resources to devote to preparing for an outbreak that may not deeply impact his district. But “if we’re fully unprepared, there will be questions,” he said. Inquirer staff writers Lizzie Mulvey, Brooke Schultz, and Aubrey Whelan contributed to this article. New York declares state disaster emergency over rising measles cases. Here's what that means - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报由于 麻疹 病例激增,纽约州州长 Kathy Hochul 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日宣布进入州灾难紧急状态,今年报告了 108 例,自 7 月中旬以来报告了 92 例,主要发生在免疫不足的农村地区。该紧急命令有效期至 2026 年 11 月 4 日,允许药剂师和紧急医疗技术人员为 2 岁以下的儿童注射 MMR 疫苗,并扩大 疫苗 的使用范围。它还暂停了 19 岁及以上个人报告 疫苗接种 时征得同意的要求。在全国范围内,今年已确诊近 4,000 例 麻疹 病例,为 1991 年以来的最高水平,其中宾夕法尼亚州有 1,000 多例病例,导致 200 人住院治疗,5 人死亡。CDC 强调了两剂 MMR 疫苗对保护的重要性,其有效性为 97%,并指出 疫苗接种 率有所下降,从而促成了 暴发。自 7 月以来,该州已注射了 1,000 多次 MMR 疫苗,旨在提高免疫覆盖率。
查看新闻五要素
时间
纽约州州长 Kathy Hochul 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日星期一宣布该州进入灾难紧急状态,该命令有效期至 2026 年 11 月 4 日。
地点与对象
纽约州,特别是社区免疫不足的 18 个农村县,以及整个美国,几乎每个州都报告了 麻疹 病例。
事件
由于 麻疹 病例数量不断增加,纽约州宣布进入州灾难紧急状态,这些病例主要发生在未接种疫苗的个人中。该声明是州长 Kathy Hochul 在审查了 麻疹 案件的最新数据后做出的。
规模、影响与风险
纽约今年已记录了 108 起 麻疹 案件,自 7 月中旬以来已有 92 起。全国范围内,今年已确诊近 4,000 例病例,为 1991 年以来的最高水平。宾夕法尼亚州已报告 1,000 多例 麻疹 病例,其中 200 人住院治疗,5 人死亡。CDC 建议使用两剂 MMR 疫苗 进行保护,一剂的有效率为 93%,两剂的有效率为 97%。
应对、状态与不确定性
该州已采取措施扩大 MMR 疫苗 管理范围,允许药剂师和紧急医疗技术人员为 2 岁以下的儿童接种疫苗。该命令还暂停了之前为 19 岁及以上人群报告 疫苗接种 时需要征得同意的要求。所有 MMR 疫苗接种 必须在 72 小时内报告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

New York declares state disaster emergency over rising measles cases. Here's what that means - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
New York Governor Kathy Hochul declared a state disaster emergency on October 6, 2026, due to a surge in measles cases, with 108 reported this year, 92 since mid-July, primarily in under-immunized rural areas. The emergency order, effective until November 4, 2026, allows pharmacists and emergency medical technicians to administer MMR vaccines to children as young as age 2 and expands vaccine access. It also suspends the requirement for consent when reporting vaccinations for individuals aged 19 and older. Nationally, nearly 4,000 measles cases have been confirmed this year, the highest since 1991, with over 1,000 cases in Pennsylvania, resulting in 200 hospitalizations and five deaths. The CDC emphasizes the importance of two MMR doses for protection, with 97% effectiveness, and notes that vaccination rates have declined, contributing to the outbreak. The state has administered over 1,000 MMR shots since July, aiming to increase immunization coverage.
Original source text (English)

OffOn Stream on New York declares state disaster emergency over rising measles cases. Here's what that means New York has recorded 108 measles cases so far this year, most since mid-July. ByMary Kekatos and Dr. Jade Cobern October 6, 2026, 6:57 PM Share Follow ABC News on Google New York Gov. Kathy Hochul declared a state disaster emergency on Monday as neighboring Pennsylvania battles its largest measles outbreak in three decades. As of Oct. 3, New York has recorded 108 measles cases so far this year, 92 of those since mid-July across 18 rural counties where communities are under-immunized, according to Hochul's order. "After reviewing the latest data on measles cases in New York State, I am declaring a state disaster emergency to strengthen our ongoing efforts to confront this disease," Hochul said in a statement. "No one should get seriously ill or die from a vaccine-preventable disease." Related Measles cases surpass 1,000 in Pennsylvania amid the state's largest outbreak in decades It marks the first time a state has issued an emergency order since measles cases began rising last year. At least 95% of measles cases in the U.S. have been among people who are unvaccinated or whose vaccination status is unknown. The order allows registered nurses to order the collection and testing of specimens for suspected measles cases. New York Governor Kathy Hochul speaks during a news conference, Oct. 2, 2026, in New York. Yuki Iwamura/AP Photo Early symptoms include cough, runny nose, fever as high as 104 Fahrenheit and red, watery eyes, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). Two to three days after symptoms start, patients may develop Koplik spots, which are tiny white spots that may appear inside the mouth on the inner cheek. Three to five days after symptoms start, patients may experience a rash, first appearing on the face at the hairline before moving downwards to the neck, chest, stomach, arms, legs and feet. Fever may spike even higher than 104 F when the rash appears, the CDC says. The order, in effect until Nov. 4, allows pharmacists in New York to vaccinate children as young as age 2 with the MMR shot. It also expands who can administer MMR vaccines to include emergency medical technicians-paramedics, advanced EMS and midwives. Additionally, it suspends a previous requirement that health care professionals who administer vaccines to those aged 19 and older receive consent before reporting vaccination to New York City or New York State immunizations registries. All MMR vaccinations of children and adults must be reported within 72 hours. Related How hospitals, clinics in Pennsylvania are responding to the measles outbreak "Department staff are working closely with local health departments and regional partners to track and contain the current measles outbreak," Dr. James McDonald, commissioner for the New York State Department, said in a statement. "The measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine is the best protection -- protecting nearly all people against disease who receive the recommended doses." Popular Reads Bend, Oregon, shooting: Suspect ID'd, had attended elementary school, police say Oct 16 France reports 1st known human case of Shamonda virus 1 day ago Man hops onto moving school bus when he sees driver in distress 2 days ago The state said it has administered more than 1,000 MMR shots in rural areas since July. Meanwhile, Pennsylvania health officials are now reporting more than 1,000 measles cases. Nearly 200 people have been hospitalized and the state has reported five measles-associated deaths, which is the most any state has reported in over 30 years. This outbreak follows a trend of growing outbreaks in the U.S. over the last two years. Earlier this year, South Carolina reported the end to a measles outbreak of 997 cases. Last year, Texas reported an outbreak of 762 cases. Measles virus particle, illustration. Kateryna Kon/science Photo Library/Getty Images Nationally, nearly 4,000 cases have been confirmed this year in the U.S., with infections reported in almost every state, according to the CDC. It's the highest number of illnesses from measles since 1991. The CDC currently recommends that people receive two doses of the MMR vaccine, the first at ages 12 to 15 months and the second between 4 and 6 years old. One dose is 93% effective in preventing measles infection, and two doses are 97% effective, the CDC says. Infants aged 6 to 11 months living in or traveling to high-risk outbreak areas may receive an early third dose of the MMR vaccine. However, CDC data shows vaccination rates have been lagging in recent years. During the 2025-26 school year, 92.4% of kindergartners received the MMR vaccine, according to data. This is lower than the 95.2% seen in the 2019-20 school year, prior to the COVID-19 pandemic. School vaccine exemptions also reached a record high last school year. Recent CDC data showed that vaccine exemptions for kindergarteners increased to 4.2% during the 2025-26 school year. This is up from 3.6% compared to the previous school year and 2.2% during the 2020-21 school year. New York has recorded 108 measles cases this year, with 92 since mid-July. Nationally, nearly 4,000 cases have been confirmed this year, the highest since 1991. In Pennsylvania, over 1,000 measles cases have been reported, with 200 hospitalizations and five deaths. The CDC recommends two doses of the MMR vaccine for protection, with one dose being 93% effective and two doses 97% effective.
Response, status and uncertainty
The state has implemented measures to expand MMR vaccine administration, allowing pharmacists and emergency medical technicians to vaccinate children as young as age 2. The order also suspends a previous requirement for consent when reporting vaccinations for those aged 19 and older. All MMR vaccinations must be reported within 72 hours.
原文 / Source
